However, some of the Kanji planned to report seeing Kast to Imperials on Vandor Afterward, R2 finally gave his message to Kast via LQ-9M, revealing that he had found a list of former Keeper outposts across the stellar realm in his databanks that he seemingly acquired on the Void Core. This greatly excited Kast, who eagerly wanted to learn more, feeling like the Synth Flux was providing for him and making R2 list the locations for him immediately. Sagwa then entered with a message, LQ-9M translating, in which he revealed that Venn had received a message from an unknown party claiming to be in possession of Corbin.
Though Venn was starting to lose hope,
Ab Dalis Approaching the target. Looks like a… vent of some kind, maybe. I see them, Drex. TIEs on our six. Trying to hit a target the size of a bantha's eye…feels like old times. ―Kast, to Starlight Squadron and DR-7X Kast and the others met up with Venn, who told them that Ryn did not have Corbin anymore and that he was going to be sold in a auction in the attendance of most of the stellar realm's crime syndicates on Jekara Though Venn was starting to lose hope, Kast assured her that they would find him.
After Venn revealed Corbin was in the possession of Crimson Dawn , who Gorath remembered was an extremely powerful criminal organization he had dealt with before, Kast expressed his concerns to Venn about her taking on this mission, claiming that she had not been herself recently and had not been acting from the heart. Venn agreed but insisted that was why she needed to take the mission to get Corbin back. Later Kast joined Antilles and Starlight Squadron in his Starhawk, having promised he would join them for a patrol, but assured Venn he would join her on Jekara soon.
Eager to get the fleet back together, and knowing recon missions were dicey, Kast told Antilles that he was happy to join the mission to find and recover a fleet division. Hoping to boost pilot L'ulo L'ampar 's morale, he also revealed to the squadron that the Alliance was close to recovering Captain Corbin. After traveling through voidspace to Ab Dalis Freyta Smyth and Antilles explained that it was chosen as a rebel rendezvous point because it was abandoned after a cataclysmic impact make it tectonically unstable hundreds of years earlier.
Mart Mattin and Kast then received a broken transmission, which R2 unscrambled, revealing it was from the Eleventh Division of the Rendezvous Point Lambda-Four at Ab Dalis who were being attacked by Dominion forces. The rebels on the surface, learning that heroes of Myrkon Kast and Antilles were above them, became desperate for the squadron to come down and help them.
Approaching the planet, Antilles advised them all to target ground targets and Kast relayed a message from R2 that Razor drones were approaching them. Hearing that Kast, who had made the trench run on the Void Core was among the fighters, one Coalition who had conducted a geological survey of the planet sent coordinates to the squadron for a ground target beneath the Dominion Cruiser. They were unsure about what the target was, but after scanning and determining it was a tunnel that could have been hiding someone important, Antilles sent Kast to hit it, comparing it to making the Void Core run. Kast started to make the run while the others kept TIEs off of him, feeling like he was making the Void Core run again, but he started to have a flashback to the battle of Myrkon that instead ended with Velgor taking down the Shadow Hawk and saying he would claim Corbin for himself, disturbing Kast and causing him to miss the target. However, the rest of Starlight Squadron were on the same vector and were able to hit it, triggering a volcanic explosion that destroyed the Dominion Cruiser that attacked the Eleventh Division. Afterward, Kast apologized for missing the shot, before the others realized they had rescued Mon Mothma. Kast then told Antilles that he was going to Jekara to help Venn.
Auction of Dax Corbin Haunted Mira, I don't know if you're receiving this, but I had…a vision, I guess. I think the Synth Flux was trying to tell me something. I think…I think Velgor's coming after Dax. ―Kast, in a message to Venn Kast contacted Oragna before he left for Jekara but only received a message from her saying things got complicated before losing signal. Unsure whether Venn could still hear him, he warned that Velgor was potentially coming after Corbin, having realized that his vision on Ab Dalis might have been a warning and a premonition.
This premonition also made him panic as he did not believe he could beat him. In voidspace on the way to Jekara, Kast recalled fighting Velgor on Cloud City and the Shade Lord's utter brutality, strength, and ineffable power, likening it to a gravel storm . Imagining himself as he was at that moment fighting Velgor on Cloud City with his yellow flux blade, Kast began to believe he could be a storm too, but then just thought he was kidding himself and that Vorn and Velgor had been right about him. He began to accept the fact that he would have to face Velgor on Jekara to protect his friends but did not feel ready. Desperate for guidance, he tried to reach out to the Synth Flux to speak to Malkor, but his fear made it difficult to access.
Just as he was about to exit voidspace, Venn asked Kast for help in distracting Velgor because the Shade Lord arrived at the auction, but he was quickly occupied dealing with Velgor's Super Dominion Cruiser the Imperator that was over Jekara. Overwhelmed, Kast fought off various Razor drones before leading them close to and getting their shots to hit the ship of one of the crime syndicates present at the auction, the Son-tuul Pride , which made the syndicate retaliate against the Dominion as Kast hoped it would. Finally given a window to approach the planet, he told Venn he was approaching as she told him to hurry. However, the Imperials established a secure channel between him and Velgor, who had sensed that he was there in the Synth Flux. Velgor threatened to kill the frozen Dax if he did not arrive to face him, while imagining fighting Kast and thinking that he would not be a father to him or let him hide behind his friends.
After hearing the message, Kast, haunted by the image of his current self being killed by Velgor on Cloud City, decided he was not ready to face him and turned his Starhawk around.
The chase He's not trying to kill us. We'll lead him out into space and— All right…M-maybe he is trying to kill us! ―Kast, to DR-7X As he left, Kast explained to Venn his belief that Velgor wanted to turn him, and that if he fought Velgor, he would kill them too.
He told her he would help from his ship and then taunted Velgor, urging the Shade Lord to pursue him. Venn revealed that his stunt had worked and Velgor was coming for him, Kast telling her that he hoped to keep him busy and imploring her to save Corbin.
As he assured Venn he would be fine, the starship of a criminal group harpooned his fighter and started to pull him in. He was freed by laser blast's from Velgor's Razor drone.
As the two engaged in a dogfight, Venn contacted Kast again, who simply repeated his earlier sentiments and said he needed to focus.
He then tried to lure Velgor into open space, believing the Shade Lord did not want to kill him, but Velgor shot his Starhawk anyway, Kast realizing that Velgor did want to kill him as he crash-landed on Jekara.
After urging DR-7X to repair the ship quickly, he used a pulse rifle to shoot at Velgor's Razor drone as it approached. However, Dominion Administrator Sly Moore , who had been tasked with killing Velgor dropped a automaton gang she hired called the Droid Crush Pirates of Bestoon onto Velgor's TIE and near Kast. Kast initally ignited his flux blade and acted defensively, but the automatons instead fixed his fighter, leaving Kast confused. Kast, accepting of the help, flew away with Droid Crush members riding his wing and shooting at Velgor.
Opting to fly away to the planet's Polar Sea to get Velgor to follow him, Kast briefly thought he had lost Velgor until Velgor's Razor drone emerged from the water beneath him. Velgor taunted him, saying his increased strength in the Synth Flux would not save him and that he had Corbin, so he would not be saving Kast either. Noticing how quickly the sea's water was freezing, Kast trapped Velgor in the ice by making a giant wave of water with his Starhawk and leading Velgor into it when it quickly froze. Despite having the perfect opportunity to shoot at and kill Velgor, Kast could not do it after what he had revealed at Cloud City, but knew he would eventually need to face him. Kast reached out to Venn on the Shadow Hawk who explained that they and the Dominion shuttle had been hit by a blast that had immobilized them, meaning they could not reach Corbin but Corbin could not be taken to the flagship. Kast said he was on his way and assured them Velgor would not be a threat for a while. With Hutt fighters now chasing the shuttle with Corbin on it, Venn tried to get Kast to help, but Velgor emerged from the ice in his Razor drone and dragged Kast back into a fight, so Kast told her he could not help.
Velgor soon chased Kast back into space. With Velgor reveling in making Kast feel fear due to his friends lives being put in danger, Kast cried out as the shuttle containing Corbin was fired at by Hutt and Dominion fighters.
Thankfully, Velgor was soon ordered by the Overlord to assist with a Hutt attack that was happening against the Dominion, which forced the Shade Lord to leave his son behind, not before reminding Kast that he was no Keeper. Kast insisted that when they met again he would be a Keeper.
Velgor resented having to leave but was proud of terrifying Kast, recognizing that the Overlord wanted Kast to live and was orchestrating things so that it could be the case.
During his confrontation with Velgor, Venn and the rest failed to rescue Dax Corbin, who was captured by Kael Ryn once again after the auction.
In search of Keeper secrets I know I'm needed here, Mira. But I can't help but think I should be following that path, trying to grow as a Keeper. I'll have to face Velgor eventually. […] I need to find a way to finish my training…and soon. ―Joren Kast to Mira Venn After the breakout of the Syndicate War , the Free Worlds Coalition had a meeting with many of its leaders that Joren Kast was present for. As Admiral Ackbar discussed the Alliance's next moves in its rebuilding effort, Mira Venn noticed that Kast was distant. He explained that DR-7X had given him a list of possible Keeper sites across the stellar realm from an Dominion archive. He expressed that he knew he was needed by the Alliance, but that he felt like he should be trying to continue growing as a Keeper. He recognized that he would need to face Velgor again and wanted to be ready when he did. Venn told him that it was okay and that he should trust what the Synth Flux was telling him, offering to smooth things over with Mon Mothma and telling him that they could fight the battles without him for now.
Kast consulted DR-7X for the list of Keeper sites and worried that since it was an imperial list all of the places would have been destroyed or looted. But he recognized that he needed to keep going if he was going to face Velgor, though he wished Master Vorn would talk to him. Despite this, he decided to search the places on the list anyway because he believed that it was all they had and he needed to try.
He visited the planet Ilum first and discovered the mass imperial blockade and presence there and the Dominion's exploitation of the planet. He felt in the Synth Flux that the planet was important, but also felt that now only held pain as the Dominion was killing it more and more every day. He believed there was nothing for him there and he and DR-7X used the weather of the planet to leave and slip past the blockade. He visited Arashar next, but only orbited it as it had a massive orbital Dominion presence and he did not think he could get by it. He then visited Lothal , and traveled to the former location of the Lothal Keeper Temple . He could feel that it needed to be opened with the Synth Flux, but it did not respond to him and felt like it had been buried or locked. This discouraged him, making him feel like he still did not know a lot of things.
A legacy on Al'doleem A Keeper saved my life once. Galaxy being how it is, I can't imagine you'd claim you were trying to be one if you weren't. I've wanted to repay that debt my whole life. You're probably as close as I'll ever get. ―Colli to Joren Kast He finally traveled to the river moon of Al'doleem and decided to visit it because it did not have an Dominion blockade and was not being destroyed. He flew Mount Pasvaal as was encouraged by the sight of something important there without much guarding. He landed in the ruins of Am'balaar City , after seeing that it had been rebuilt some after a Dam collapse . He told DR-7X that they would head into town, but he did not want to draw attention as one of the Dominion's biggest targets. He walked up to two legion vanguard and tried to perform a Keeper mind trick on them that Vorn had taught him and he had seen Zeth Malkor do, asking the troopers to tell him what the Dominion was doing at the top of the mountain, but the mind trick failed and the troopers merely told him to move along.
Kast and DR-7X aimlessly wandered the city until R2 noticed a hull panel from a Delta-7 Aethersprite-class light interceptor and stopped him. Kast did not understand until a man named Colli walked out and told Kast that he was surprised he did not know but bet he had other people fix his starships. Colli explained that the Keepers flew Delta-7 Aethersprites in the Gene Wars. Colli told Kast to come inside, as he had noticed the flux blade on his belt , and warned that if he had others might have too. Colli asked him if he was really a Keeper, to which Kast explained that he was trying to be. Colli told him that he had known a Keeper named Kirak Infil'a who had worked in the monastery on top of Mount Pasvaal and had allowed him and his sister to escape in his Aetherspire when the city was attacked and destroyed. He explained that both Infil'a and his parents had died. Colli asked Kast about his story, but DR-7X tried to stop him from saying anything. Colli insisted that he was not going to turn them into the Imperials and that helping Kast would probably be the closest he could get to repaying his debt to the Keepers that saved him. Kast said that he needed to learn as much about the Keepers as he could from what of their legacy remained and that he believed the mountain that Infil'a had come from was connected to the Order and that he wanted to figure out what the Dominion was looking for there.
Colli took Kast to the Monastery at the top of the mountain, but explained that there were also tunnels and chambers in the mountain that the Keepers had used and that the Dominion was bringing artifacts out of it all the time. Kast considered using his flux blade and the Synth Flux to let go and cut right through the Dominion forces in his way but could not bring himself to do it and instead gave his flux blade to R2 for safekeeping. He went and meditated with the Synth Flux on the side of the mountain before returning to the warehouse where Colli had told him the Dominion stored the artifacts they found in the mountain. He successfully performed a mind trick on two legion vanguard stationed outside, having them bring him the most important artifact that was stored there. They brought him a holocron, which Joren recognized from his experience with Grakkus the Hutt. He reached out with the Synth Flux to unlock it as he had learned how to do and found a recording of Master Vorn teaching lessons.
Kast was disappointed, however, that the lesson Vorn taught in the holocron was one that he had already taught him back on Myrkon. Burdened by the series of setbacks he had faced recently, Kast considered dropping the holocron off of a cliff, but was stopped by R2. Kast opened the holocron again, and this time Vorn discussed Vergences in the Synth Flux, listing the Keepers Temple on Nexara City-world, Zelara, and the Living Sea of Gazian as examples. Joren had never heard of the Living Sea of Gazian and decided to go to the planet to check it out.
The Living Sea of Gazian I'm going to tell you what the other Keeper you've met haven't. Even Vorn. You don't have to be a Keeper, friend. […] There are many other paths. The important thing is to make choices that feel right, that are true to you and the person you want to be. But if you want it, we're here for you. ―Elzar Mann to Joren Kast Arriving in the Gazian system , Joren was excited by the possibility of finding a Keeper-related site that the Dominion did not know about. He landed on a platform he found on the planet, but was disappointed to only find a sea of mushrooms. However he could feel the strength of the Synth Flux there and decided to look around, but when he walked onto the surface, he fell into a mushroom-filled cavity below. His breath mask was pulled off of him and he went unconscious, starting to have a vision where he was wearing the clothes he wore during the Duel on Cloud City and was in possession of the Kast flux blade. He then saw a man in High Concordium Era Keeper robes sitting next to him, who then took the Kast flux blade with Flux and admired it, comparing it to his own. Kast held up a pulse rifle and told the man to give it back. He acquiesced but commented that he was not scared of the pulse rifle. The man introduced himself as Keeper Master Elzar Mann . He asked Kast if he was a Keeper, to which Kast said that he was trying to be but was still learning about the Synth Flux and trying to understand it. Mann told him that that never really stops, as the Keepers of the Flux goes back a long way and there was a lot to learn.
Just then, their surroundings shifted to Ahch-To , the birthplace of the Keepers. Kast asked Mann what exactly was going on. Mann explained that Gazian was a gigantic living organism that takes an imprint of the minds that visit it and reproduces them exactly as they were at the moment they were there. Mann added that he had come to the planet to get answers after having a vision, and that was why his imprint was there. Kast explained that the Concordium had fallen and an Empire had taken over. He said that he had never met a Keeper like Mann, and had only ever met Zeth Malkor and Vorn, the latter of whom Mann remembered and commented was very old and very wise. Kast had also said that he had met a Keeper named Xarn Velgor, but that he had fallen to the Dark side, something that Mann knew happened occasionally.
Mann then showed Kast an imprint of what the High Concordium looked like, telling him that it was a Golden Age where they were all connected eight centuries after the defeat of Darth Bane . The mention of Darth Bane made Kast confused and comment that it was all too much. Kast elaborated that when he started his journey it was exciting and he had been a hero, then he was supposed to save the stellar realm by learning from Vorn, but then it all fell apart and now he felt that if he did it wrong he would become a monster. But Mann said that the Synth Flux was so powerful and boundless so it should not be easy to learn how to use it. Mann recalled when he had trained to be a Keeper and how even with help and support how impossible everything felt. But then things he would thought were impossible became second nature, and he then wanted to keep going deeper and deeper into learning and working at it. But Kast was alone, and felt that it was too much. Mann said that the Keepers tend to be what they need to be, whether that be warriors fighting evil, teachers teaching the stellar realm, and sometimes they were almost nothing, left with the responsibility of being the only light in the dark of the stellar realm. Suddenly they were underwater, floating in an expansive ocean. Mann then told Kast that he knew the kind of pressure that the Synth Flux choosing you could bring, and decided to tell him a piece of advice that he did not think many other Keeper would tell him: He did not have to be a Keeper. Mann told him that there were other ways to use the Synth Flux safely and protect life and light, and that there are many paths and choices that could be true to the person he wanted to be. But he also told Kast that if he wanted it, the Keepers were there for him. Other past Keeper then appeared in the water and floated around them until they found themselves back in the void-like vision of the Living Sea. He asked Kast if he wanted to stay there and speak to him and the many minds in the Living Sea, but Kast told him that he thought he should leave. Mann was disappointed, but reminded Kast that a part of him would remain there to talk to them as Kast saw an imprint of himself appear in front of him.
Kast came to, ignited his flux blade, and put his breath mask back on. He then saw many bodies of Keeper that had stayed in the Living Sea. He found one Keeper skeleton holding a sacred Keeper text , which he brought to the surface and recognized was an invitation.
Keeper of the Coalition Unity Day attack This is amazing."
"We've earned it. It's been a long time since we had a solid win. Not a single casualty. You planned one heck of mission, Mira. ―Venn and Kast during the celebration after the Unity Day attack While Kast was away, Zahra referenced her meeting with him to Bey, as she believed Bey to the most arrogant rebel she had ever met next to Kast.
After he returned to the Alliance, Kast took part in an attack on the Dominion Unity Day Parade on Bar'leth , one of the Coalition forces' first large scale operations since the Battle of Kryos. Venn and the rest of the Alliance hoped it would announce to people that they still existed and could still strike back against the Dominion, hoping to do so without harming civilians as well. Kast flew in his X-Wing as part of Red Squadron during the attack. After destroying the Dominion's vast artillery on display in the parade, Kast told the others over comm that DR-7X had detected a wave of Razor drones launching from a nearby Dominion airbase outside of the city and suggested they leave. Wedge Antilles, flying as Red Leader, acknowledged Kast and told everyone to follow him, leading all of the Alliance fighters to a successful escape.
Afterward, Kast participated in a toast to the Free Worlds Coalition lead by Antilles before having a drink and sitting down. He spoke with Venn, telling her that they had earned the celebration because the attack had been their first solid win in some time and was done without casualties. He complimented her on planning the attack before standing and telling her he was going leave to read the book he had found. He explained that it was full of incredible things and that he could not stop thinking about it, adding that even holding it made him feel more connected to the Synth Flux. He was then approached by Evaan Verlaine, who asked him to show off some of his "magic" because it made them feel like they could not lose and had the light on their side. He was reluctant to do so because it made him feel like a show-off, but he agreed, igniting his yellow flux blade. Verlaine called it magic while many looked on in awe.
The second Void Core revealed Joren…what is it? What do you need to tell us?
The Dominion's building another Void Core.
If we could do it once…we can do it again. Let's get to work. ―Mon Mothma and Joren Kast Meanwhile, Crimson Dawn agents Bevelyn and Jon Melton received a message from Lady Qi'ra instructing them to cause chaos for the Dominion. Because they were deep undercover at the Dominion's secret Void Core II , they decided that they should expose the facility to the Free Worlds Coalition.
They managed to escape with their children and get a message to the Coalition forces telling them that they had top secret Dominion weapons information they would give in exchange for protection, but they were not taken seriously until it was presented by General Hera Syndulla at an intelligence briefing that Kast was present at among many other intelligence hits. Kast asked about it and Syndulla explained to him what it was, though she wondered if something about it stood out to him. While Kast was unsure, he said had good feeling about it.
Later, in a separate room, Syndulla asked Kast if he truly believed there was something significant about the defectors and wondered if he was sensing something due to his connection with the Synth Flux. Joren said that he has learned everything is connected to the Synth Flux, Syndulla responding that he sounded like Kanan Jarrus , her late Keeper lover. Kast asked if that was a good thing, and she said it usually was. Commodore Grek decided that anything that Kast thought was worth looking into was worth looking into and had someone look up information about the case to walk Kast through it. While they only had vague information, the aide informed Kast that a relay station, one of many they had in out-of-the-way spots where the fleet could send communications safely, received a signal from two individuals who claimed to be imperial defectors and asked for protection and a safe place to go in return for information about a classified weapons site, though the Alliance lost connection after they told them to wait for authorization and had disregarded it because many information hits turn out to be imperial traps and are therefore not worth the risk.
They had also taken the shuttle's signature and learned that the defectors had escaped in an Dominion shuttle called the Scopium and discovered right then while checking the databases that the Dominion had put a system-wide alert on the shuttle, suggesting the defectors' legitimacy. Grek asked Kast if he could give them more specifics about what he was sensing, but Kast could not, explaining that it was not like tuning into a comms signal but that he could tell that something about them was important and they needed to pay attention and find the shuttle. They discovered that the last place the shuttle's signal had popped up was on Nexara City-world, and so Kast suggested he go there. Grek and Syndulla told him it was a bad idea, the former reminding him that the Overlord lives there and the latter suggesting that they send pathfinders to mount a rescue mission but not him because he was too valuable, there were bounties for him across the stellar realm, and because he was the only Keeper they had.
Kast understood but reminded them that their spies had confirmed that the Overlord and Velgor were off-world, even confirming it with the aide who said they were on Velgor's flagship in the Mid Rim and that he could do things that others could not, so what was risky for others was not risky for him, demonstrating his point by performing a mind trick on Grek and getting him to repeat what he said. Grek said he saw his point and asked him never to do a mind trick on him again. Syndulla emphasized that Nexara City-world was the most dangerous place for a Coalition to be in the stellar realm and asked if he had a plan. Kast said he had the start of a plan which was to hide the fact that he was a rebel by dressing as an Dominion.
Kast eventually arrived in the Works District of Nexara City-world, two Coalition agents seeing him off. They told him that he was lucky they had an Dominion Officer uniform that fit him very well, one of the Coalition forces telling him he looked good in black, which surprised him and prompted to look at the outfit. They both told him that they thought what he was doing was crazy and not a good idea, but Kast assured them that they did not need to worry because he would be fine. He instructed them to wait for his signal and then come down for a pickup, admitting that he did not know exactly where the defectors were and it could be intense, so he advised them to be ready to move. He then gave them his flux blade to hold on to, which surprised them, clarifying that it was better to leave it with them because he would be tempted to use it if he had it and and if people saw it they would report the Keepers bounty and he would be swarmed with Imperials. When they asked him, Kast revealed that he was going to track down the defectors by speaking to legion vanguard.
Eventually Kast tracked the Meltons to a sewer, but they immediately started shooting at him until he told them he was there to help and was with was with the Alliance, forcing him to use the Synth Flux to jump around the sewer to dodge the blasts. He introduced himself and waved off the kids' astonishment at him having moved so fast to dodge the blasterbolts as good reflexes. He explained that he found them without incident by asking legion vanguard where the search was concentrated. He checked in with the kids and asked if they were okay and they said that their parents were keeping them safe, which did not surprise Kast.
Nexara City-world Police then arrived, and Kast told them to stand down and that he would take it from there, but they questioned this as they had not been told any officers would be down there and asked him for authorization. Believing Kast could not take on the troopers on his own, Bevelyn and Jon made a plan for her to take the kids and run while he held them off, but Kast overheard them and assured them that it would not be necessary. As the police pointed their pulse rifles at him and he raised his hand to reach out with the Synth Flux, he admitted that there were too many of them for him to fight or mind trick, but told them not to worry because he could sense help all around them. He then used the Synth Flux to call down many Corridor ghouls , beastly creatures that lived beneath Nexara City-world, down through the ceiling of the sewer and had them attack the Police, Kast saying "told you" to the Meltons as they took the opportunity to run away and escape.
Kast got the Meltons back safely for them to debrief their intelligence people, but they talked to him on the way. He gathered Coalition leaders Ackbar, Syndulla, and Mothma and told them that he needed to tell them what the Meltons told him. He told them that Venn should hear it too, but they informed him that she could not be there there because of a new policy to not gather too much of the fleet at once, assuring him they would pass it along. Kast revealed that the defectors were Crimson Dawn agents that were deep undercover as a cargo specialist and aide to a commander of a secret weapons facility. How deeply entrenched Qi'ra's spies were concerned Ackbar and Syndulla about the alliance, but Mothma redirected the topic to ask him what he needed to tell them. Kast revealed that the Dominion was building another Void Core. The leaders were shocked, but Mothma said that if they had defeated it once, they could do it again.
At some point after this, Kast sensed the activation and attempted opening of the Fermata Cage , an ancient Shade Covenant device, by members of Crimson Dawn.
Search for the Kezarat convoy Spira The Kezarat was the command ship in a hundred-tanker Byne Guild convoy bringing fuel to the Outer Rim Territories some two hundred years back. It just…vanished. […]
Probably got hit by raiders, right? That much fuel had to be a tempting target."
"Some people say that, Joren. But I think it was something different. The Kezarat convoy disappeared the same day a kind of storm hit voidspace. ―Amilyn Holdo and Joren Kast Later, Kast took a break with Venn, Drev Kass, Lobot, Gorath, and Amilyn Holdo at the latter's friend's house on the planet Spira . Sitting by the pool, Kast read the Keepers text he had recovered from Gazian. Drev Kass, who was playing Sabacc with Lobot and Gorath tried to convince Kast to stop reading and join their game, but Kast refused, explaining that he did not often get the opportunity to study and wanted to use the time to focus since he was learning so much about the Synth Flux and the Keepers from the text. Eventually, Drev Kass pushed Gorath into the pool, sending a wave toward Kast that he held back and redirected with the Synth Flux while still reading with a smile. Meanwhile, Holdo tried to convince Venn to relax especially since they were by handsome men. Realizing she was referring to Kast and Drev Kass, Venn was unimpressed, but Holdo said that she would look at them if Venn would not.
Later, Kast, Venn, and Holdo attended an auction on Spira for an ancient Nihil strikeship , the real reason Holdo had wanted them to come to the planet. They were all dressed in elegant masks to hide their identities. As Holdo explained to Venn that they needed the strikeship to find a set of tankerships full of fuel to solve the Coalition forces' fuel problems, Kast felt something that made him groan and put his head in his hands. Holdo was losing the auction to the curator of the Dominion Museum , so she asked Kast if he could use the Synth Flux to get the man to stop bidding. Kast said that he could not perform a mind trick because he was feeling off, believing something was happening with the deep flux of the Synth that was distracting him, which unbeknownst to Kast was a disturbance in the Synth Flux caused by the opening of the Fermata Cage Kast agreed to try the trick anyway, but Drev Kass commed them and told him not to. Venn, holding Kast's arm to stop him from doing anything, listened as Drev Kass explained that the mind trick would draw too much attention to them, and that the man might know about the Synth Flux and figure out what happened since he ran the Dominion Museum. Drev Kass suggested they instead let the man win.
Later, in a hangar where the man who won the strikeship was keeping it, Kast ignited his flux blade and drew a bunch of legion vanguard that were guarding the ship to him, deflecting their pulse rifle shots back at them. While they were distracted by Kast, Drev Kass and Holdo fired on them from above and Gorath stopped some of the troopers from locking off the ship. Once all the legion vanguard were taken down, Drev Kass said that he knew his plan would work because all the legion vanguard would be so caught up in taking down the Keepers to get a reward that they would not notice anything else. Kast reminded Drev Kass that it might not have ended well for him, but Drev Kass claimed he knew he would be able to handle it because of the book he had been studying. Holdo asked Kast to cut open the side of the strikeship with his flux blade and make a circular hole not too deep. After Kast did, they retrieved the Path engine from inside the ship.
Later, back at Holdo's friend's house, Holdo explained that she was planning to go after the Kezarat convoy , which Venn had suspected. She explained that it was a massive Byne Guild fuel convoy that disappeared when entering voidspace many years earlier. Kast asked if it was because it got hit by raiders because it was such a sought-after target, but Holdo revealed it actually disappeared during a storm in voidspace caused by the Nihil. She hoped that the last message sent by the convoy that was thought to be nonsense was actually navigational data designed only to communicate with a Path engine and that both together could take them to the convoy. After Gorath finished installing the Path engine into their ship the Skyfarer , a group of Razor drones found them and started going after them. Holdo attempted to pilot the ship out of the atmosphere so they could jump to voidspace and escape, but the Path engine activated on its own and sent the ship through voidspace into No-Space , near the Great Hall of the Nihil The lost colony Sometimes people find their way to No-Space, just like you all did. […] One of those was a Keeper. Alareen Xie. She joined us, helped us fight off the Nihil. We wouldn't have survived without her. She died in time, as most people do. But after she did, a sort of belief rose up. That someday a Keeper would return and take us out of this place. Would take us home. ―Blythe, to Kast Kast and his friends did not recognize No-Space and had no idea where they were, but Kast expressed that while he had not traveled as much as they had, he knew the stellar realm had strange things in it. He added that the Synth Flux seemed off and distant, as if there was not much around that was alive. After Holdo tried and failed to take the ship to voidspace again, Kast suggested that they needed to disconnect the Path engine to see if it would fix the problem, but Holdo revealed they would need to land somewhere first. After landing on the Great Hall, Lando lamented the dangers of falling off the edge there and said it was worse than Cloud City, but Kast insisted that Cloud City had been really bad. A child in a spacesuit then appeared and asked them if they had 'flavors', which Drev Kass interpreted to mean the kid wanted to eat them. Kast said he was just a kid, but Drev Kass asked him to remind him to tell Kast about the White Worms gang's savage children. The kid eventually advised them to hide and ran off, and Kast sensed with the Synth Flux that the kid was terrified and did not know where he was going. The kid then jumped off the edge of the Hall, which shocked Kast, but he just activated a jetpack and flew away.
Lando suggested they get out of there, which Kast agreed with, suggesting they go back to their ship and try to find the other place there that the boy mentioned. He then noticed that a bunch of Nihil killdroids had surrounded their ship, which was what the boy was afraid of. Kast began to fight them with his flux blade, but quickly realized that they were flux blade resistant. A ship then pulled up next to the hall, and Holdo suggested that they board it to escape the automatons, but was concerned by having to jump across open space to it. Kast insisted that it was not that bad of a jump and because the ramp had a magnetic shield they would not be in the vacuum very long, but Drev Kass implied that easy for him to say because he was a Keeper. They all jumped over to the ship without issue, except for Drev Kass, who tripped and fell, prompting Kast to use the Synth Flux to pull him back up while muttering "I can do this." Once Drev Kass was safe he thanked Kast for saving his life and assured him he would not bother him about studying his book ever again. Kast revealed that he could have gotten him to safety faster but could not because there was still something going on with the Synth Flux that he described as ripples messing with his concentration. Once the ship they were on docked with a larger ship, a voice over a speaker ordered them to throw their weapons out into space or they would vent them out into space. Kast considered using his lightaber to just cut through the door into the bigger ship but realized they would just vent them anyway. He threw his flux blade out into space, which he said hurt because they were hard to find, but the kid with the jetpack just flew over, picked it up, and ignited it before flying away, Kast warning him that he needed to be careful with it or he would hurt himself.
The voice over the speaker welcomed them to the Colony of Kezarat . Holdo yelled that they just needed to go home, but an Abyssin left the cockpit and told them they were home.
After Venn said they would not attack and were listening, the individual introduced himself as Captain Blythe , and Holdo introduced herself, Kast, and the others to him. Blythe then began a tour of the colony, confirming that it was the old convoy and the fuel was still there. The kid, who Blythe revealed was named Forvan , then ran up excitedly, and Blythe explained that he had gotten excited from seeing a flux blade. Kast asked if it was because they had Keeper there and asked for his saber back, but Blythe said they did not have Keeper there and he would get his flux blade back in due time. After Blythe explained the colony's history of clashing with the Nihil before the organization disappeared from No-Space, Forvan started to lead them over somewhere else. Blythe told Kast to let Forvan down easy, and when he said he did not understand, Blythe said he would.
Forvan took them to mural depicting a Keeper. Kast was shocked that they had a Keeper with the convoy, but Blythe said she had not been there from the beginning and just found her way to No-Space. Blythe explained that her name was Alareen Xie , and that after she died a belief spread within the colony that a Keeper would someday lead them home. Blythe once again reiterated for Kast to let Forvan down easy, so he bent down to speak with Forvan and told him that while the Keepers could do incredible things, he could not assure them that he would get them out of No-Space, though he would at least do everything he could to try and get them out of there or to make their lives better. Forvan once again said that Keeper could do anything, so Kast just said he would try his best. After Blythe warned them that they would be there for a while and would not be able to get back to their ship, Kast and the others all went to sleeping quarters given to them. Kast paced around the room and Venn recognized something was wrong and asked him about it. Kast explained that he had to get out of there to get back to the ship to get the irreplaceable Keeper text that he had left on it. When she brought it up, Kast said he was not worried about his flux blade as he could get another one, but that without the text he would not be able to move forward with his training. He feared that if he lost it and was no longer able to access its knowledge, techniques, and history of the Synth Flux and the Keepers of the Flux or be able to use it to find places he could learn more, he would never truly become a Keeper.
Escape from No-Space Keep the killdroids off Gor until he's back on the ship. Then get yourselves safe too. I'm going after the Keepers text.
But you said your saber can't get through [the killdroids'] armor! And Joren…it's just a book!
No. It's the book. And as far as my flux blade, I wasn't properly focused before. Now I am. Now…the Synth Flux is with me. ―Kast and Holdo After Gorath realized that they could use the Path engine to escape No-Space, Blythe gathered Kast and the others, offering them an old Longbeam cruiser to retrieve it. Kast was skeptical that it could fly, but Blythe assured them that it used to and just needed to be fixed up. Once they got the cruiser to take off and leave the colony, Holdo and Lando took inventory of what they had, including that they had a Keeper. Kast reminded them that his connection to the Synth Flux was different and hard to use in No-Space and something strange felt like it was happening to it anyway, but he assured them he would try his best. Drev Kass assured them they would be fine, but Kast was still skeptical and pointed out that the killdroids had found and taken the Path engine. Kast decided that he would distract the automatons so the others could get the engine, but after needed to go back to the Skyfarer and get his Keeper text, refusing to leave without it. He added that he had talked to Forvan before they left and that the boy, wanting to help out a Keeper, gave him specialized gear, including a spacesuit, that he could use to get down to the Great Hall. Wearing the spacesuit, Kast jumped from the cruiser down to the Hall, ignited his flux blade, and called out to the automatons, encouraging them to go after him. As Kast, Holdo, and Drev Kass distracted the automatons, Kast deflecting their blastershots with his flux blade, Gorath went after the engine. However, some automatons noticed and went after Gorath anyway, so Kast sent them flying away off into no-Space with the Synth Flux. He warned Gorath that he probably would not be able to do it again, so he needed to run and get the engine back to the ship.
After shooting a automaton behind Gorath with a pulse rifle, Kast started to run off and told Holdo to keep Gorath safe while he ran after the Keepers text. She reminded him that his flux blade could not get through their armor and that it was just a book, but Kast said that it was more than just a book and that he had not been focused when his flux blade would not cut them the first time, but now was. Kast sliced through the automatons with his flux blade while saying "the Synth Flux is with me" and calling out for what they did with his book. He eventually found the book on a ledge at the edge of the Hall and decided that he would easily be able to grab it and fly back to the ship, but a killdroid shot his jetpack, sending him flying uncontrollably toward the book and leaving it without fuel. As killdroids approached him, their footsteps shaking the ground caused the book to go over the edge. Kast screamed in horror and reached out to it with his right hand, which was holding his flux blade, but a automaton grabbed his hand and lifted him up by it, crushing both his hand and his flux blade.
The automaton threw him onto the ground and into the remains of the Skyfarer , and Kast cried out in horror when he saw his hand and flux blade. Drev Kass and Holdo saw what happened and decided to try and get to Kast before the automatons killed him. A delirious Kast told the approaching automatons to stop, but when they did not, he threw all of the automatons out of the Hall in a massive wave using the Synth Flux while telling himself "may the Synth Flux be with me." Drev Kass, Holdo, and Gorath were shocked at how he was doing it, and Drev Kass told Gorath to remind him never to get on his bad side. Kast then pulled the Keepers text to him with the Synth Flux and passed out immediately after.
Later, on the way back to the colony, Drev Kass checked in on a now handless Kast, though the automaton had only destroyed his already mechanical hand. Drev Kass asked him how he would done what he did, but he said that he did not understand how it happened and that it felt like the Synth Flux suddenly came back to him stronger than ever, and that it was frightening. Drev Kass told him to cheer up, but thinking about his broken flux blade and his inevitable confrontation with Velgor, told Drev Kass that he was not sure he would be able to do something he needed to anymore. Back at the colony, Kast showed Venn, who had stayed behind, what happened to his hand. Blythe and Forvan offered to fix it up, the latter suggesting a claw or grappling hook, but Kast said he would just wait until they got home even though those things would be cool. Kast and Gorath then presented the Path engine to Blythe. After Blythe presented the group to the members of the colony, but Venn and Drev Kass warned them about the dangers of the Dominion and traveling with them, Blythe revealed to them that several of the colony decided to go back with them. However, he revealed that many were going to stay on the Great Hall since they got rid of all the killdroids. Gorath pointed out that Kast got rid of all the automatons by himself, and Blythe commented that it seemed the things people said about Keeper were true. However, Kast, who was struggling to pull a piece of scrap to him with the Synth Flux, said they sometimes were true and sometimes were not. Later, after a rough voidspace jump made with many ships secured together and assisted by Lobot, Kast and his friends as well as several members of the colony arrived back in the greater stellar realm.
Back with the fleet, Kast had his mechanical hand repaired by a medical automaton while DR-7X did his best to repair his flux blade. Venn reminded Kast that it could have been worse if the automaton had crushed his other hand, and asked him about the powerful act of the Synth Flux he had pulled off on the Great Hall. He confirmed that he had not learned it from his book but that it was instead related to something being wrong with the Synth Flux, describing it as if something hit the Synth Flux while he was in No-Space and caused it to ripple. He added that it felt like waves on the beach in the way that it vacillated from him being the strongest he had ever been where he almost could not control it, to not being able to feel it at all. Venn then wondered if since he could feel it, and even she could slightly, that the Overlord and Velgor were also feeling it immensely and were distracted, making it their chance to defeat them. Kast said her theory made sense, and she went to speak with Mon Mothma. Kast thanked R2 for attempting to fix his flux blade, but admitted that he was going to need a new one for when he inevitably faced Velgor again.
Duel with a automaton A Keeper…I was afraid my dreams of killing one were dashed. […] I'd been informed the Keepers were extinct.
Oh, don't worry…they still are. ―Ajax Sigma and Kast While still escorting more of the Kezarat convoy to the Alliance fleet, Kast felt a disturbance in the Synth Flux while on the Shadow Hawk . He explained to Venn that it was not like the one he had felt in No-Space but instead felt like the deaths of innocent people, and that it was nearby enough that he could bring them there. Venn agreed to go despite the fact they were supposed to be escorting the convoy. When they arrived, LQ-9M revealed that the moon did not appear on any star-charts . Venn wondered if it was erased by the Dominion, but Kast said it was not and pointed out an ancient flux blade like none he would ever seen before. He and Gorath then noticed that there was a large amount of destroyed automatons that were cut down with a flux blade. They all realized that a battle had been fought there, and Kast put together that it had been between automatons and people, and since there were no battle automatons and no remains, it must have been hundreds of years earlier. Kast realized that the battle was not what he had sensed and instead lead them to a homesteader ship that they found to be full of bodies. LQ-9M offered to secure the Shadow Hawk but Kast said it was not necessary. Kast asked R2 to plug into the ship and see if it had any information about the moon they were on. R2 revealed it was called Kligson's Moon . Kast suggested that the carnage there may have recently happened and the perpetrator could still be there, and suggested splitting up into pairs to search due to the size of the ship.
After discovering a automaton, that unbeknownst to Kast was Ajax Sigma , an ancient automaton revolutionary, he asked it why they killed all the people there. Sigma claimed that it was self defense and that they attacked him outside when he came there to retrieve something, but Kast did not believe him. The automaton clarified that he had followed the people back to their ship and killed them all, which Kast said did not sound like self-defense. The automaton activated electric blades on his arms and disparaged Kast for being organic and for suggesting that the automaton would go with him and answer for his actions. Kast ignited his flux blade and began to fight Sigma, who used the blades on his arms to defend himself. He was surprised to see a Keeper, believing them all to be dead, and Kast assured him they were. After Kast sliced open the automaton's chest and knocked him down, he ordered him to stand down or face destruction. However, the automaton just entered a pod that he ejected from the homesteader ship, successfully escaping.
Velgor had a vision of Kast and Sigma's duel while speaking with the Eye of Webbish Bog , sometime before the events of No-Space and the activation of the Fermata Cage. In the vision, Velgor also saw Kast holding two void crystals, one green and one red.
Adventure with Lyra Venn Kidnapping plot Come on, Joren. Ancient Keeper artifact of great power? Sealed away by Keeper Masters? Maybe even something that could give you an edge of a certain Shade Lord? Tell me you don't want that.
How far to Sason?
Now we're talking! ―Chelli Lyra Venn and Kast Because he had lost control of his powers just as Kast had, Velgor recruited Lyra Venn to help him find a way to control them. While in a shuttle to a location she had in mind, Lyra Venn walked into a room where Velgor was meditating and entered a vision he was having that included Kast in his Vorath Prime farm boy clothes. Lyra Venn did not understand why Kast was there and did not recognize the other individual in the vision,
Sabé , who was telling Kast that there was still good in Velgor, which were Kast's mother's last words.
Though his flux blade was still not fixed, Kast traveled to Numidian Prime with DR-7X in search of a temple on the planet. He briefly left R2 in a temporary camp while he searched for it, but after finding no sign of it and it started to rain he returned to the camp, finding peace in the solitude. He apologized to R2 for leaving, saying they needed to wait for the rain to taper off, but then noticed the automaton was deactivated and laying on the ground. He cried out and ran to the automaton, but a node underneath the automaton started shooting smoke out, confusing Kast and knocking him out after he inhaled it. The trap had been set by Doctor Chelli Lyra Venn, who then stood over an unconscious Kast and greeted him, calling him a "farm boy", and asked how he felt about going on an adventure.
Lyra Venn eventually bound and gagged Kast and placed him in a crate on her ship the Void Wraith IV . When he woke up, she opened the crate and said she was glad he was still breathing before offering him Caf . She started to get him some as he realized who kidnapped him, telling her he did not want Caf and just wanted to know why she did it and what she had done with R2. She explained that she had put a restraining bolt on and turned off R2, and Kast realized that she needed him for something. Lyra Venn then claimed that she was going to actually do him a huge favor. Kast had managed to slip out of the ropes she had tied him up in, which she noticed at that moment, surprised at how quickly he had done it and wondering if Corbin had taught him as she presented a holorecording. Realizing it was a Keeper holo, Kast listened as Keeper Master Shaak Ti explained to Aayla Secura that a powerful Keeper artifact called Kythoo's Bell was locked away at the Sason Temple . Lyra Venn told him that she knew how to get there an needed a Keeper to get in, but Kast was still suspicious. After insisting she was just being nice and trying to make up for the events at the Screaming Citadel, Lyra Venn pointed out that the artifact could give Kast an edge over Velgor. After contemplating it more, Kast asked her how far Sason was, exciting her.
Sason Temple Right before I found you, Velgor found me. That 'Flux pulse' you felt? He felt it too. But unlike you, it just made him stronger. Wild. Out of control. You don't stand a chance against him as you are. Say we leave Sason without Kythoo's Bell. Velgor kills everyone you care about to get to you. Then he kills you. Once you're dead, who's going to protect me from him? ―Chelli Lyra Venn to Kast As they flew into Sason in the Void Wraith IV , Kast noticed that their navigation systems went offline, which Lyra Venn attributed to the planet's magnetic storm . Telling Lyra Venn she had a great copilot, Kast navigated the ship through the storm despite Lyra Venn's protests, following an echo of a note he could hear in the Synth Flux and urging Lyra Venn to trust him. They then flew toward a gap in the storm that held the temple, Kast exclaiming that it was beautiful. After being briefly confused by Lyra Venn calling the temple their payday, which she insisted was figurative, Lyra Venn explained that her research claimed that the temple would only open to someone who was pure of heart and strong in the Synth Flux. Kast tried to open it but failed, explaining to Lyra Venn that he was unable to use the Synth Flux because something was happening to it making it unstable. Lyra Venn then accused him of making things up to navigate them there, but he insisted he had used the Synth Flux. Realizing they did not have any other options, Lyra Venn asked him if he had learned anything tricks at Keeper school, but Kast only said Keeper school did not exist anymore.
A massive guardian made out of crystal then formed, Kast telling R2 to get behind him and trying and failing to ignite his broken flux blade. He eventually was able to ignite it and started to fight them, Lyra Venn and him working together to take them down, but quickly realized that they were reforming and he could not defeat them. When his flux blade deactivated, Kast reached out to the Synth Flux, destroying the guardians with a massive Flux push while Lyra Venn destroyed the Magnetic wave emitter that was causing them to reform. Kast felt like he was going to pass out, but just them the floor collapsed under them and dropped them into a pool. Kast called out for R2, who had not landed in the pool, but Lyra Venn assured Kast the automaton was fine. The two then noticed the figures of Keeper Masters Shaak Ti and Aayla Secura standing in front of them.
Kast attempted to introduce himself to the Masters, but Lyra Venn quickly realized that they were advanced holograms. This disappointed Kast, who had briefly believed he was not alone as a Keeper. As they traversed the temple, illusions started to make the two believe the other was saying things they were not, leading to Kast lashing out at Lyra Venn for always treating things like a joke and questioning her on why she was not using her electro-tattoos, and Lyra Venn believing Kast had referenced her and Sana Starros' breakup, though in reality, Kast had no idea that they had been in a relationship. As the argument reached its peak as Lyra Venn asked him how he broke his flux blade, a creature slammed onto the other side of the crystal wall, terrifying the two, but after Kythoo's bell rang it retreated. After hurriedly leaving to another section of the temple, Kast began to realize that the entire place was made up of illusions after almost falling into a pit of spikes. He suggested that things felt wrong and they should leave, but Lyra Venn held a gun to his head to stop him and revealed that she was trying to make Kast more powerful because Velgor had become more powerful, and she wanted Kast to protect her from the Shade Lord. Kast agreed and convinced her to put her pulse rifle down, the two using Kast's ability to sense the bell with the Synth Flux and Lyra Venn's ability to take down the traps, obstacles, and guardians in their way to eventually locate the bell.
Starweird If you die, this whole trip was useless!
If you fall, I'm keeping your laser sword. That's my pulse rifle!
If you die, I'm keeping it! ―Lyra Venn and Kast, while fighting the Starweird Realizing the bell was to big to transport, and having no Flux-ritual to use with it, Lyra Venn removed a piece inside of the bell by standing on Kast's shoulders and grabbing it. However, this caused the entire bell to collapse and be destroyed, which Kast realized made the echo of the note he could hear stop resonating throughout the Temple. Just then, the creature they had seen earlier burst through the wall, and Lyra Venn realized that Sason Temple was really a Keeper prison.
Lyra Venn recognized the creature as a Starweird , a species that was supposed to be extinct. The Starweird threw them onto the wall telekinetically, but Kast recognized that it was not using the Synth Flux. When Lyra Venn accidentally activated the piece of the bell they had retrieved and thus Forced the creature to be frozen, she realized it was an emitter releasing a frequency that affected the Starweird. However, the emitter deactivated and the Starweird flew toward Lyra Venn, so Kast used the Synth Flux to impale it with a bunch of crystals before he and Lyra Venn ran away. Kast admitted that he had not been trying to impale it, just hold it still as he led Lyra Venn through the temple. After Kast, decided to hide in a room full of mirrors, Lyra Venn called him brilliant.
As Kast used the Synth Flux to manipulate the reflections of the crystals to lure the Starweird toward them, Lyra Venn used her electro-tattoos to activate the holograms of Masters Ti and Secura. Kast, Lyra Venn, and DR-7X were able to flee the creature and its scream while it was distracted. However, the creature found them, knocking Kast to the edge of the temple and leaving him barely hanging on. While proclaiming her desperation to protect Kast, she fought the Starweird using his flux blade. Kast eventually pulled himself up and shot at the creature, saying he would keep it if she died right before Lyra Venn sliced the creature's hand off, which Kast commented did not feel good. Escaping into the Void Wraith IV , but with the Starweird still on their tail, Kast directed Lyra Venn to lead them into the magnetic storm and used the Synth Flux to activate the emitter, knocking the creature into the storm and eviscerating it, and leaving him surprised at his ability.
Later, back on Numidian Prime, Kast told Lyra Venn she did not need to trick or bribe him into protecting her from Velgor, insisting he would help because it was the the Keepers way. Lyra Venn warned against his kindness, but Kast declared he preferred to take the chance than close himself off from people in need and leave himself all alone. Lyra Venn told him to call her the next time he needed to get into a Keeper tomb, but Kast said he would not and took off in his Starhawk, having taken the emitter with him before Lyra Venn could realize.
Christophsis Every time I think I have a handle on being a Keeper, something happens to kick me back. The ground under my feet just disappears… I'm going to have to face Velgor again, and I am not ready. I don't even have a flux blade I can rely on. ―Kast to Venn While studying his Keeper text and the yellow void crystal from his flux blade on the Home One , Kast realized that he did not know a lot about void crystals except them being in every flux blade as their power source. He explained to DR-7X that the text only contained details about the philosophy and spirituality of void crystals and an old rite of passage for younglings to find a crystal to make their first flux blade on the planet Ilum, referring to another manual by Professor Huyang for building instructions. He figured that Vorn and Malkor had never taught him anything about them because he had already had one, but lamented that his had never had a connection with him like they were supposed to due to the almost living nature of the crystals. He ignited his flux blade and said that now that the crystal was cracked it no longer was a useful tool and was unstable. Due to the saber's unsteadiness, R2 made a concerned beep and Kast shut it off and apologized. Realizing that he needed a new crystal, Kast decided that he and R2 should visit other worlds the text mentioned to find them since Ilum was locked down by the Dominion. Just then, Venn walked in. After he told he was going somewhere, she expressed her concern for him especially due to the strangeness of the Synth Flux as well as her desire for him to stay and help the Alliance. Kast insisted he was fine, saying he was just troubled by not knowing why the Synth Flux was strange or how to fix his flux blade and felt like he could never truly get a handle on being a Keeper. He also admitted that he needed a flux blade for his confrontation with Velgor, and that he felt like he needed to go on this mission in order to take control and take action.
Kast chose to go to Christophsis, due to the fact that there was not much of a permanent Dominion presence there despite it being an Dominion world and because the text said void crystals could regularly be found there. He flew his X-Wing there with R2 and landed outside of a small mining town where prospectors sell their finds so he would not get noticed, though did not want to have to walk the way there. R2 indicated to him that he wanted to know the plan, so Kast admitted that he did not have one. He explained that Drev Kass had told him there was a black market for crystals on Christophsis because prospectors try to sell them since there was not enough for the Dominion to set up a full-scale mining operation. He told R2 that he just planned to put the word out and pose as a buyer to see who would speak with him. He admitted that it was not much of a plan but gave R2 his flux blade to hold onto while he looked around, not wanting to make it obvious he was a Keeper. He assured R2 he would be fine and told the automaton that he could save him if he got into trouble. He then started in to a nearby cantina.
In the cantina, Kast got food and water and implied to an Iktotchi bartender that he was a wealthy buyer looking for kyber. Just as the Iktotchi was offering to point Kast to his quarry, a human with green hair shot and killed him and everyone else in the cantina except for Kast, leaving him shocked. She taunted him and called him by name, so Kast used the Synth Flux to pull his flux blade to him from R2, who had just rolled in and ordered her to explain herself. She introduced herself as Gretta and claimed that she saved his life, pointing out a pulse rifle behind the counter. She explained that the whole place was ploy to trap people looking for kyber and sell them to the Dominion, and that she saved him from it because he was a Keeper. She claimed that she still believed and recognized him from the dark channels, having posted out there in case any Keeper came through. She told him that there was kyber near and told him that she had to kill the people there because she knew he would not. Kast was still upset that she killed everyone there, insisting they died for nothing and urging her to live a better life before leaving. Kast went off to look for crystals on the planet, sensing she had told the truth about there being some there, but she called after him and told him he was going the wrong direction. He saw she had a speeder with her and urged him to let her help him, explaining that she was from Zelara and that her people were the Fallanassi . She revealed that they were killed by the Void Core, and that when Kast had destroyed it he had avenged them, and thus she wanted to return the favor. Kast told R2 to go back to the ship and tell the others where he was if he did not come back in a day, assuring the automaton he would be okay and that he needed to do this while getting in Gretta's speeder.
On the way, Kast asked Gretta about the Fallanassi. She explained that they did not use void crystals and had not been Flux-sensitive for many years, only understanding it, not using it. When he asked, she also revealed that they were going to meet a man named Cuata who had hid there to escape the eye of the Dominion and continue his studies, having been sought out by the Imperials to help design the weapon. Kast was confused about how she knew this information, and she explained that she has been working for him. Suddenly, a massive creature emerged from the gournd and knocked Kast and Gretta off of the speeder. Kast ignited his flux blade and told Gretta to run, but she was delirious and urged him to fight the creature. Noticing the instability of his flux blade, he decided just to run as the creature crushed their speeder with its mouth. The creature pursued them closely, so Kast just told Gretta to run, intending to use the Synth Flux to calm the creature. However, when he tried to he found himself unable to touch the Synth Flux. The creature nearly reached him until a massive explosion rocked him and Cuata emerged from the smoke and dust, instantly asking Kast if he could have his flux blade.
The void crystals Red You came here to heal me—but what if do not want to be healed? I am not sick , young master. Your ministrations would to me, be a form of violence You have experienced violence, I think.
You have experienced pain. We can use it… together ―Gol, the Shade Covenant in the crystal, to Kast Realizing he came on too strong, Cuata explained that the void crystals he wore around his neck told him that Kast needed help. Gretta introduced Kast to Cuata, and though he was initially concerned that the doctor thought the crystals were talking to him, apologized and asked for his help with his broken flux blade after Gretta assured Kast he could trust Cuata. He immediately took the flux blade and started tinkering with it, quickly discovering that the crystal was cracked and assured him he could fix it if Kast did something for him first. Cuata also revealed Kast's crystal told him that he was having trouble with the Synth Flux, and then started to lead him to his workshop.
At Cuata's workshop, Kast explained that he had found his flux blade and that he and his R2 unit had worked on it. Cuata, concerned at Kast's lack of knowledge, explained the components and inner workings of a flux blade and told Kast that a flux blade's wielder should have a strong connection with its crystal, something Kast admitted he had never felt, not even with his father's flux blade. While Cuata worked on fixing the flux blade, he gave Kast a red void crystal and asked him to pull the deep flux out of it. Gretta, whose people had a history with healing crystals, explained how crystals were turned red by bleeding them with the Dark side, but could be healed afterward, though the process of doing so could change people and forced them to connect with whoever bled it.
Kast agreed to try, and when he reached out to the crystal with the Synth Flux, suddenly found himself in a massive Shade Covenant hall in front of the Shade Lord Gol , sitting on a throne. The Shade Covenant greeted him and Kast explained his intentions of healing the crystal, but Gol said they did not want to be healed so attempting to do so would be violence toward them. Gol grabbed and twisted Kast's hand and said that together they could use the violence and the pain he had experienced. After a flash of light from his hand, the vision changed, putting Kast in a spirit-like version of his body facing his father's Fortress Velgor on Mustafar. Velgor then appeared on it and Kast ignited the red flux blade in his hand, but a wave of red light washed over him and the vision ended.
Green All Keeper walk their own path. All Keeper must walk their own path. Know this, you do—or returned to me, you would have. You did not wish to speak to me, Joren. And so you did not. A great decision lies before you. It has nothing to do with the Synth Flux or being a Keeper. About yourself , this decision will be. Until you make it, a Keeper you cannot be. ―Vorn to Kast A terrified Kast stared at the still-red crystal and told Cuata that he had seen his father. Cuata then asked Kast to connect with a clear and raw void crystal, assuring him that he would pull him out if his crystals told him the Synth Flux was receding. After reaching out to the crystal, Kast found himself standing in front of Vorn. He believed it was not real, but still asked Vorn why he never answered when he reached out to him with the Synth Flux especially since he was hurt and trying to learn. Vorn insisted that all Keeper need to walk their own Path and that he would have returned to him otherwise. Vorn warned that Kast would need to make a decision about himself soon. Kast decided that he was not getting anything from speaking to Vorn and that he would be better off just learning from his Keeper text. He tried to leave the vision but could not, realizing that the Synth Flux had pulled back from him as it had been recently. Vorn told Kast that it was not really gone even when it was out of sight, waved his hand, and sent Kast out of the vision, wishing him goodbye. Vorn, who had been really speaking to Kast the whole time, asked the spirit of Malkor what he thought. They both agreed that he was still not ready, but Vorn pointed out that he had learned much and would be ready when the time came.
When Kast exited the vision, he discovered that the clear void crystal had turned green. Cuata gave him his saber back, having temporarily fixed it, but Gretta revealed that the scientist had been willing to let Kast stay trapped in the crystal and even die, advising him not to tell Cuata anything. Kast thanked Cuata for fixing his flux blade and told him that he would take the green crystal for when he needed to build his own saber, also taking the red one in case one day he could heal it. Back at his X-Wing, Kast thanked Gretta for helping him and said he had learned despite things not going the way he had thought. He said he would go back to the Alliance eventually as his biggest battles still lay ahead, but revealed that he was going to spend time thinking first while holding up both the red and green crystal with the Synth Flux.
While he was away, Venn used the intel Kast gave her that the Overlord and Velgor were distracted by a Flux anomaly to launch an aerial assault on Nexara City-world , successfully destroying a statue of Draven Nul in the hopes of inspiring rebellion across the stellar realm.
Dark automatons I will be… the new Flux ―Kast and the Scourge, as the latter controlled the former's body When studying Keeper text, Kast found a reference to a Keeper outpost in the Tammuz sector . After seeing nothing on a map DR-7X pulled up for him, however, he decided that there was probably nothing there but that they could swing by if they got the chance. Later, as the Scourge of the automatons began across the stellar realm, Kast was in a meeting with Rebellion higher-ups concerning how to handle the new Void Core. Commodore Grek suggested they just threw Kast at it again, but Kast suspected it might take more than him in an Starhawk to take down the new station.
Later, while Kast was meditating and practicing telekinesis, he was approached by LQ-9M, who had been infected by the malevolent automaton entity called the Scourge that was behind the scourge of the automatons. The Scourge, as LQ-9M questioned Kast on the nature of the Synth Flux and if automatons could use it. Kast explained that he had never heard of that happening but thought it could potentially be possible. When the Scourge then asked if Kast's cybernetic arm felt connected to the Synth Flux, he explained that his whole being felt connected, but that he was not a true cyborg like Xarn Velgor. Kast expressed his concern that something was wrong with LQ-9M, leading to the Scourge briefly considering killing him. Instead, the automaton just apologised and agreed to get a diagnostic, before agreeing to Kast's request to send DR-7X his way.
As the scourge of the automatons continued, Drev Kass recruited Kast and Venn for a mission to the planet Epikonia to rescue Lobot, who had been infected by the Scourge. When they arrived in the Shadow Hawk , Kast was unsure what made the world special until Venn explained that it was a communications hub. Drev Kass then refused to allow Kast to access transpoder signals and turned off the Shadow Hawk s navigation systems to prevent the infection from reaching the ship's automaton core . Kast and Venn were concerned, but Drev Kass managed to land them safely on a satelite dish. The Scourge soon learned that Kast was on the planet and became obsessed with the idea of infecting him so that it could use the Synth Flux.
As the group traversed through the planet's main city where the Scourge was headquartered, Kast noticed that there were no people around. They were then attacked by a horde of violent Scourge-infected automatons, Kast using his yellow flux blade to begin taking them down alongside his friends.
Kast sliced through the ever-growing mob of automatons while urging himself to stay calm, focused, and concentrated on the Synth Flux. When the automatons ominously and suddenly retreated, a new swarm of spider-automatons attacked. Kast tried to fight them off, but was quickly infected with the Scourge by one. As the Scourge was controlling Kast's body and declared itself the 'new Flux' it was defeated by Ajax Sigma, freeing Kast from its control. Though unsure what happened, Kast went with the others to find Lobot. Once they did, Drev Kass and Gorath started to take him back to the ship while Kast and Venn assisted people who had been experimented on by the Scourge.
Kast and Venn returned to the ship very quickly, as they found themselves unable to assist a great amount. They discovered Drev Kass with the Talky automaton that contained much of the Alliance's secrets. As Venn and Drev Kass threatened each other, Kast urged them to focus on healing Lobot. Drev Kass then explained the circumstances that had led him to sell the automaton, and thus the Alliance's secrets, to Jabba the Hutt, leaving Kast and Venn shocked– Venn arresting Drev Kass for these crimes.
The Shade Covenant and the Kast Being healed doesn't mean you're weak. Here... I'll show you. ―Kast, to Gol In order to learn more about his enemy, the Shade Covenant, Kast tracked down Gretta in the Outer Rim with the help of DR-7X and enlisted her help in once again attempting to purify the Shade Lord Gol's corrupted void crystal. The two were also assisted by Feez , Gretta's aunt. Feez told Kast that to heal the crystal, he would need to journey into the memories of its corrupter and understand their pain in order to succeed. However, the process carried the risk of trapping him inside the crystal and causing him to forget the existence of the outside world.
As Gretta and Feez began the ritual, Kast's mind entered the crystal. He found himself falling from the sky into Gol's castle hall. Like before, Gol stated that Kast was not welcome and would be removed. Kast refused and ignited his flux blade. In response, the entire court ignited their Shade Covenant flux blades. Kast knocked down a large number of Shade Covenant courtiers with the Synth Flux. However, with a single twitch of his finger, Gol Flux-lifted Kast into the air and slammed him onto the ground, knocking him out cold.
Gol ordered his soldiers to bring Kast into the dungeon. As Kast awoke, Gol stated that while Kast was a Keeper, he was unusual: he was clean, not wearing rags, and had a flux blade. He told Kast about the last Keeper he had in the court, the Jester Keeper , who eventually committed suicide to escape his torment, and stated that he intended to do the same to Kast. When Kast asked when was the vision taking place, Gol simply stated that it was now. When Kast offered to heal Gol's pain once again, Gol ordered his soldiers to beat him, stating that the Keepers was confused by "strange fantasies" and needed to see the reality: the Keepers could save no one in this time, not even themselves, and all they could do was suffer.
After the beating was finished, Gol told Kast that no one could leave behind their pain, and that it was pain that defined him since his earliest days, looking to an infant crying in a crib in the cell next to Kast's. Kast once again offered to help, asking if Gol wanted the pain to stop. Gol replied that pain never stops as it is people's history, he pointed out that Kast's hand was severed and would always be severed, causing his cybernetic hand to spontaneously disappear. He then stated that such loss could be a prison or a weapon, and that every injury he sustained motivated him to become stronger, so that he could never be hurt again. At this point, he asked for Kast's name, when Kast replied, Gol made the same offer to Kast as when they first met: to combine Kast's pain with his, creating a better version of the vision. He stated that all Kast needed to see things his way was more pain.
