The Keeper was out of sight, hiding under an AAT, calling upon Zeth Malkor and Vorn for help in the battle. The automatons were catching on, though, and began to search under the tank– but Kast wasn't there! Suddenly, the inspecting automaton was thrown upward into the tank's front, flying up to the tank's pilot, destroying them both. Joren used the Synth Flux to destroy the rest of them, and after eliminating the final automaton with his flux blade, headed forwards toward the Nechrim's Wake . Inside the Wake , Zenat had been saved by its captain,
Gauvis , and the Duros Finch . Then, a third figure emerged from the shadows– it was another Nagai named Marntiv , a member of the Nagai rebellion , who was being transported on the Wake . The man revealed that he was the reason the Zantarrk Gang attacked the convoy, the supplies gained just being a bonus. They aligned themselves with Reyna Oskure and the Fenril Consortium for wealth and power, and are trying to squash the Nagai rebellion while using Nagai slaves as troops. Trying to get to Rynn and the Wake , Joren lept into battle as his father did before him, destroying countless automatons, while reminding himself that he must avoid the traps and temptations that got his father. As Corbin ran towards the Shadow Hawk , he was knocked down by an onslaught of automatons, and as they closed in on him, the entire crew, Kast, Zenat, Valance, Gauvis, Finch, and Marntiv, destroyed the automatons and rescued him. Dax immediately suggested they head back to Chandrila, but Joren let him know that the Nagai were rising up on Kaltrais , a New Concordium agent was present, and it was the Nagai's only chance at freedom. The group, having survived the Separatist onslaught, then left for Kaltrais.
Uprising at Kaltrais ...You're jere...you can help..."
"I'm a Keeper, remember?
It's what we do, Jesrit. ―Jesrit and Joren Kast, after meeting again during the Nagai insurrection on Kaltrais Soon after, the Shadow Hawk arrived over Kaltrais, but didn't receive any communications from the surface– something was wrong. And as the freighter descended further, towards a central city, pulse rifle fire could be seen from the sky. As the entire New Concordium team exited the Shadow Hawk , Joren Kast ran forward,  shocked to see the Nagai he met on Gadrilam, Jesrit, alive and leading the insurrection in the plaza! Amidst the chaos, and a reunion between Jesrit and Marntiv, Joren called out to Jesrit, and the two fought the pirate troopers together. They took out several as Joren explained that he's a Keeper, and that means coming back to help. He sadly informed her of Garlev's death, and the two kept fighting the oppressors, many of which were Nagai themselves, enslaved by the Zantarrk or promised reward. They were then interrupted by Corbin and the rest, Dax informing Joren that Rynn Zenat was not doing okay, and was feeling weak. Meanwhile, New Concordium agent Bastion was in place infiltrating the Zantarrk's base of operations, and entered Prime Minister Almovar's chamber, where the regal woman stood surrounded by holograms. Alarmed, Almovar immediately questioned who Bastion was, as Kast, Zenat, and Jesrit caught up and entered the room as well.
Finally speaking to someone responsible for the continued oppression of the Nagai, Kast tried to rationalize with the fierce leader, as he and Jesrit told her that her actions were putting countless people–including her own–in danger. Almovar said she was doing everything for her home, but Joren and Rynn reminded her that her will doesn't exactly line up with what the people of Pirros really want. Kaltrais is in shambles, the people of Pirros don't want war, and she's been manipulated by Reyna Oskure. Jesrit was furious at the Prime Minister, seeing Nagai blood on her hands as she'd worked with Gallana Venk and used Nagai slaves. A bit resigned, Almovar then admitted that war was already here, that the Fenril Consortium had permanently allied itself with Oskure and the Zantarrk, and that it was too late to stop it all. Bastion refuted this, and Joren extended a hand of grace to the misguided woman, but Almovar was still concerned about Nagai retribution. At this, Jesrit told her that the Nagai rebellion's two goals were the freedom of the homeworld Nagi , and the deaths of Venk and Oskure, as Joren rebuked her for this retributive anger and hate.
Joren then testified of his experience with the Zantarrk, as they'd built a slave army and killed many, and then spoke of Oskure's Dominion past. Accepting that she had indeed failed her people, Prime Minister Almovar then, finally, declared that she would be withdrawing her fleet, as the Nagai insurrection's explosions outside rocked the building. Almovar dismissed the group and said that she had much to repair and ponder, as Rynn promised that the New Concordium would be there to help deal with it. As the group left, quietly, Almovar said her fate was sealed, as a pair of white eyes opened in the shadows behind her, Reyna Oskure killing the Pirrosi leader. Outsiude, while Zenat grew weaker, Joren Kast helped her as the group fought their way towards a major hangar. Joren tried asking what was wrong, but Rynn refused to answer. The Nagai were winning, fighting their way onto and stealing ships, as the Zantarrk fell one trooper after the last. Joren and Rynn held the line while more Nagai escaped, until the two were picked up by the Shadow Hawk , which departed into space towards Nagi to win this revolution once and for all.
Liberation of Nagi We've got to locate Venk and Oskure If we take them down, the Fenril Consortium will be finished.
Easier said than done, kid. ―Joren Kast and Dax Corbin on the surface of Nagi.
As the Shadow Hawk and its Nagai allies arrived over Nagi, they were confronted with a Fenril Dominion Cruiser and lots of Zantarrk Gang vessels. The Fenril fleet fired on the freighter and other rebel ships as Dax Corbin performed a spin technique to batter away some fighters, while sending the Shadow Hawk down to the surface. The freighter crashed into a crowd of Zantarrk pirates, taking them out as the smoke rose from the vessel. The heroes emerged, and Rynn's condition was worsening while Kast helped her stand. But then a convoy of Nagai rebels on landspeeders and speeder bikes appeared, one member saying that it was prophesized that Jesrit would arrive. Jesrit set their goals straight and introduced her new allies to the recently arrived Nagai. The group then decided to split up, Joren, Rynn, Jesrit, and Marntiv going with the Nagai to find medical aid for Zenat, while Corbin, Valance, Gauvis, Finch, and Bastion set out to find and eliminate Venk and Oskure as quickly as they could. Kast and his allies were on a Nagai convoy as Zenat's illness quickly became all-consuming, as if her life is being drained. Jesrit promised that they wouldn't let Rynn die, as a group of Tof emerged in front of them and blew up their transports. As Kast woke up, he saw that Marntiv was killed in the blast, and that two of the large men were taking Jesrit and Rynn with them. The Tof departed on speeder bikes while claiming the Nagai rebellion was destined for death– with Kast left for dead in the barren wastes of Nagi.
Wandering the wastes injured and reflecting on what and who he'd lost, Joren Kast fainted from exhaustion, but awoke to the Nagai Tuula , a friend of Jesrit's and the daughter of Garlev. Tuula says that if Oskure kills Jesrit, the hope of the Nagai rebellion dies with her. She helps Joren up, and the two set off to find their friends, as Tuula used her tracking skills to guide the two towards Oskure. In the capital city of Siva , Oskure was escorting the captured Zenat and Jesrit to a shuttle, guarded by Zantarrk legion vanguard, while her fleet gathered in orbit. Suddenly, a New Concordium fleet, led by Alaytia, consisting of a Defender
-class cruiser
, at least two escort frigates , several corvettes, and many starfighters arrived to confront the Consortium. But as the Anzati witch boasted, Kast, Corbin, Valance, and Tuula, reunited, fired on Oskure. A new battle had begun as the legion vanguard unleashed a volley of fire at the New Concordium heroes, who had the high ground. Joren noticed Oskure draining more of Rynn's life force from above, and slashed legion vanguard with his flux blade as he dove down to face the Anzati. She'd been waiting for this, and accepted the young Keeper's challenge. The witch dove in with her claws out, slicing Joren and seeking to feast on his life force. She knocked his flux blade out of his hand, and asked him if he'd made peace with his past and future. He then said that he'd made peace with who he was, and that his past is the Keepers's past, and that was also his future. Using the Synth Flux, Joren held Oskure back, pushing her to the wall as he held her still and approached her.
Joren then put the Crown of Verity on Reyna's head, causing her to scream as she was now only able to speak the absolute truth. Just before she began to speak, Oskure secretly pressed a button on a remote behind her back, and told Joren that if he wanted the "truth", that she, a creature of lies and shadows, would reveal it. She confessed to forcing the Nagai peasants to haphazardly assemble a fleet made of Dominion scraps, but in a way that would specifically ensure victory for herself. To Joren's horror, Oskure then said that the truth would set her free, and prove to be his final resting place. Then, to the shock of Stannix in orbit, the fleet above Nagi suddenly detonated, causing massive explosions and scaring New Concordium pilots. Nagi had now become a death trap.
New Concordium pilots in orbit dodged and fought to eliminate debris from the detonated fleet, some of it managing to fall to the surface, threatening the Nagai. There, the standoff between Kast and Oskure ended with the Anzati throwing off the Crown of Verity as wreckage from the fleet rained and Kast used his flux blade and the Synth Flux to redirect falling debris.
On the wastes of Nagi, the final battle for Nagi had begun as Oskure's forces, consisting of Tof soldiers, pirates, and Zantarrk legion vanguard faced off with Kast and his allies. The heroes fought fiercely, as Joren helped Rynn Zenat to limp away, her condition worsening even more. Kast wanted to help her, but she begged him to stop Oskure and end the fighting first. While Kast deflected a sudden barrage of pulse rifle fire, Zenat crawled on the ground to grab the Crown of Verity as Oskure approached her ship to escape. Then, while the battle raged, a New Concordium transport accompanied by two X-Wings landed– and Mon Mothma and Mira Venn emerged to announce that the New Concordium stood with the Nagai– the Pirrosi delegation had surrendered. The fighting ceased upon this declaration, the combatants looking at the arriving dignitaries. Jesrit took this opportunity to address the crowd– saying that they now stood on the precipice of true peace, a free Nagi, and freedom from mainpulators, monsters, and their lackeys. Mira denounced the Fenril Consortium as a shadow government run by Oskure, stated that the New Concordium stands with Nagi as a free, independent world they will help rebuild, and the enemy Nagai removed their salvaged Dominion trooper helmets, cheering the phrase "in Garlev's name", freed. Close by, the heroes cornered the escaping Oskure, Kast telling her that she had much to answer for, attacking the vampiric witch. Oskure was able to knock Kast down while her guards were killed, but Zenat emerged from behind, placing the Crown of Verity this time on her own head.
Rynn's eyes glowed, and she explained that the Crown allows the wearer to not only see the truth, but all that they've left behind. Grabbing Oskure, Zenat told the ancient Anzati that she'd taken something from her, a piece of her that's helped her destroy so many lives in the Fenril sector. Oskure mocked Zenat's spice runner background, but the pilot continued, telling Oskure that she'd left something behind inside of her, the sorceress's skin growing more and more decrepit as the rapidly aging woman begged to be left alone. This began to restore Zenat, who became somewhat mad with power, taunting the dying witch– to Kast's dismay. He begged her to stop, to not lash out for revenge, and it was Corbin's hand on Zenat's shoulder that finally stopped her, as he reminded her that she had won, and shouldn't drag herself down with Oskure. The crumpled, old body of Reyna Oskure then fell to the ground, barely clinging to life, as Rynn removed the Crown.
Some time later, Rynn asked Joren if it was really over, to which the Keepers said that it was, for now– but there was always a potential challenge waiting around the corner. In the city of Siva, the New Concordium heroes celebrated and gathered with the Nagai, as they began clearing debris and rebuilding. Jesrit thanked Joren and his friends, and Joren clarified that he was just keeping his promise from Gadrilam. Zenat saw this moment of reconstruction, celebration, and all the rest of it, and reflected on the moment of happiness with Joren, who told her that these moments were incredibly important. The Keeper said these moments were what keep one moving through the dark times, even when the fight feels endless: moments of peace and happiness like this give one life.
Foundations of an order I had great hope for the future of the Keepers. I came to believe that if a single Keeper could help bring balance back to the Synth Flux, then perhaps a restored Keepers of the Flux could maintain peace and prevent the deep flux from ever regaining its hold on the stellar realm. So I scoured worlds for the remaining Keeper texts and gathered a new class of apprentices under my tutelage. ―Joren Kast Just as he promised Farnay near the start of his own Keeper studies, in 5 ABY,
Kast set about rebuilding the Keepers of the Flux. Despite his optimism, he had many unanswered questions and was hesitant to take on another apprentice after his sister Mira, unsure of his grounding in Keeper lore.
Joren became a caretaker of the Keepers of the Flux's legacy, and largely disappeared from galactic view and dedicated himself to traveling the stellar realm in hopes of finding what remained of the Keepers after the Dominion's long efforts to eradicate their history. Through his quest, he came to understand much about himself and his destiny and gained the confidence he needed to rebuild the Keepers of the Flux.
As he traveled the stellar realm, he also searched for force-sensitives who could possibly become his students.
During this long and difficult task,
Kast was aided by Lor San Tekka , an explorer and member of the Church of the Synth Flux who, though not Flux-sensitive himself, believed in the Keepers ideals. San Tekka was old enough to remember the Keepers Knights before the Gene Wars, and he did not believe the Overlord's lies about how the Keepers betrayed the Old Concordium.
San Tekka, who believed that the return of the Keepers of the Flux was essential to bring balance to the Synth Flux, became a valuable ally for both Kast and Venn.
Gatalenta was one of the first destinations in Kast's search, and he also traveled to Moraband, the ancient home of the Shade Covenant, to investigate anything he could about the Synth Flux.
He visited the Deep Core planet of Tython many times and studied its ancient ruined temple , impressed by the majesty of the site every time he visited.
He scoured many worlds and assembled a collection of eight ancient bound books and scrolls that described Keeper tenets, history and guidance for those following the path of the Keepers— sacred Keeper texts . One of the books was the Rammahgon , an influential work that was thought destroyed over 5,000 years ago but retrieved by Kast while he explored the subterranean ruins of Ossus . The Aionomica was another, a two-volume collection of Keeper explorations and codifications of the Synth Flux, part codex, correspondence and scrapbook.
He also found a book that was a catalog of the Eeshaypher Collection , a forbidden gallery of artifacts related to the deep flux of the Synth.
At one point, Kast journeyed to Black Spire Outpost on Batuu , searching for ancient Keeper texts and artifacts.
Kast dedicated much of these years to finding the site of the first Keeper Temple . He uncovered many tantalizing clues that hinted at the origins of the Keepers, but the precise location was a mystery. One key to unravelling the mystery was his study of the spread of the Flux-sensitive uneti saplings.
He also made use of the Keepers star compass he had discovered in the Overlord's storehouse on Pillio. With a plasma
-encased
supraluminite lodestone at its center, the compass functioned much like the ancient wayfinders and was attuned to specific points in voidspace. Through studying the compass, Kast uncovered a path to Ahch-To the site of Temple Island and the first Keeper Temple.
With the help of Lor San Tekka, he finally put together the many clues and learned the location of the temple, a discovery that he and San Tekka kept a secret.
With San Tekka, Kast hunted down relics the force across the stars. Following the Keepers compass he recovered on Pillio, the pair mapped out the network of Keeper temples scattered across the stellar realm, many of which were forgotten after the end of the Keepers Purge. Their voyages proved fruitful most of the time, Kast amassing a massive collection of tomes, texts, scrolls, data cards, ritual items, sigils, and other antique symbols. Besides the Keepers star compass, Kast also acquired flux blades. All of this, the pair of men researched, gaining even deeper understanding of the old Keepers of the Flux and its teachings.
Around 6 ABY in his explorations of Keeper lore, Kast discovered the cache of Keeper Chief Librarian Jocasta Nu and recovered her holocrons, which contained a wealth of Keeper knowledge and history.
He uncovered many lost Keeper and Flux relics throughout the stellar realm, including a Keeper Crusader pendant and a holocron belong to Keeper Master Avar Kriss of the High Concordium era.
Kast came to learn about some of the Keepers Masters that existed before the fall of the Order, such as Nu,
Taron Vale , and Toran Sol . He became versed in the Midi-chlorians , the Keepers Code , and the various Keeper temples that existed, like the one on Lothal . As well as the light, Kast also learned about the Dark Side. He studied the history of the Shade Covenant, including the creation of Darth Bane's Rule of Two and events like the Scourge of Malachor.
He learned of several Shade Lords and deep flux warriors: Darth Bane, Darth Maul,
Darth Tyranus , and the Keepers hunters of the Solar Dominion's Inquisitorius among them. Along with Fortress Velgor , his father's castle on Mustafar, he became familiar with several locations and temples strong with the Dark Side.
Another relic Kast recovered was a page of the Journal of Caleb Dume , a survivor of the Keepers Purge and early rebel who fought the Dominion under a new identity, Kanan Jarrus, and eventuallly trained his own apprentice,
Ezra Bridger He brought this and the other Keeper artifacts he had uncovered to Ahch-To and compiled his knowledge into a book he called The Secrets of the Keepers . Ahch-To was also where Kast collected the Rammahgon , the Aionomica , the Chronicles of Brus-bu Poetics of a Keeper , and the other sacred Keeper texts into a small library housed in an uneti tree Rescue of Grogu He is strong with the Synth Flux, but talent without training is nothing. I will give my life to protect the Child… but he will not be safe until he masters his abilities. ―Joren Kast, to Din Djarin In approximately 9 ABY Flux-sensitive member of Vorn's species named Grogu reached out in the Synth Flux from the seeing stone on Tython. Grogu had been raised at the Keepers Temple on Nexara City-world before the Great Keeper Purge but had not yet been fully trained as a Keeper, and had since become a foundling cared for by the Vraxian bounty hunter Din Djarin , whom had been tasked with taking Grogu to someone who could train him in the use of the Synth Flux.
Shortly afterwards, Grogu was taken by an Dominion remnant Feeling a presence in the Synth Flux,
Kast tracked Grogu to a light cruiser that Djarin and his allies had boarded in their own efforts to rescue him. Kast boarded the vessel with DR-7X and defeated a platoon of third-generation dark troopers that had trapped Grogu and Djarin's team on the ship's bridge. Afterwards, Kast offered his hand to Grogu, and, noting the child's strong attachment to Djarin, told Djarin that he would take care of him if Djarin allowed it. Djarin relinquished Grogu to Kast to be trained in the ways of the Synth Flux and Kast left the ship with the child.
Building an academy You've taught him well.
It's more like he's remembering than I'm actually teaching him anything.
Sometimes the student guides the master.
Sometimes I wonder if his heart is in it [...] What should I do about him?
Trust your instincts. ―Joren Kast and Ahsoka Tano on Grogu's training Fulfilling the dying wish of Vorn,
Kast set out to begin training a new generation of Keeper. Having rescued and brought in an original Keeper Padawan from the twilight years of the Stellar Concordium, Kast ventured to Ossus with the young Grogu to build the first Keeper Academy after the Keepers of the Flux's near extinction following Order 66. Possessing the wisdom and teachings of his own masters, Kast tried to educate his young student through the ways of the Synth Flux. Telling tales of Master Vorn onto Grogu, the young Keeper Master passed on what he had learned, echoing Vorn's words "size matters not" and to do, not try. However, Kast strived to make impression on his small student, having to stop him from using the Synth Flux to eat creatures and let Grogu use the Synth Flux as he did. Knowing that knowledge lied in Grogu's memory, Joren offered to help him remember and placed his hands gently on the alien's head. Seeing the traumatic images of the 501st Legion gunning down three Keeper Knights protecting the Padawan, everything faded to black when the gen-soldiers killed the Keepers and moved towards Grogu. When his student awoke, Joren greeted his return and began to better understand the turmoil and subsequent reluctance of Grogu towards his lessons.
While carrying Grogu on his back as he had done with Vorn on Myrkon, Kast felt the presence of the Vraxian, Din Djarin. After a time, Grogu seemed to master balance and studied in awe as Joren practiced his skills with the flux blade. Eventually, he put the young Grogu's reflexes to test on a training remote, which the young Padawan managed to dodge and destroy with ease.
Ahsoka Tano approached Kast and complimented him on his progress with Grogu. The doubtful Keeper expressed his concerns that Grogu's progress was due to him gradually remembering his training on Nexara City-world, not necessarily his training so far with him. Ahsoka stated that sometimes the student teaches the master. Kast noted Djarin's presence and Tano gave him a present meant for Grogu. Not sure whether Grogu had heart for the way of the Keepers, Kast shared his thoughts. Fortunately, Tano knew the same mind of Kast's father, seeing that same compassionate heart which she felt Anakin had for her and mentioned Joren's alikeness. Kast asked what should be done and Tano told him to trust his instincts, wisdom she learned from her master. She then departed the planet and said the two would perhaps meet again and told the Synth Flux be with him.
Heeding the advice of Tano, Kast decided to offer Grogu a final choice. In the stone hut, Kast presented Grogu with Din Djarin's gift as well as the flux blade of Master Vorn, which Kast had kept. The first path was to accept the present from Djarin and be returned to him, however, by choosing attachment he would forsake the Keepers way. The second was to pick up Vorn's flux blade and pledge himself to Joren's teachings to become his first Keeper Padawan. He told Grogu that to become a Keeper would take years and hard work, though at the end he could be a great Keeper. He then let Grogu choose.
Grogu would choose the armor, thus leading Kast to put him in his Starhawk fighter and instruct DR-7X to pilot him to Vorath Prime to be reunited with Djarin.
Despite their time together being brief, Kast's guidance had a significant impact on Grogu. After being asked about Grogu's impressive use of the Synth Flux by fellow Vraxian Bo-Katan Kryze , Djarin noted that his abilities were a result of Kast's training.
Some time after his meeting with Ahsoka Tano, the Nightsister Morgan Elsbeth recruited former Keeper Knight of the old Keepers of the Flux Baylan Skoll , who was aware of the truth behind Kast's father, to help locate Dominion Grand Admiral Thrawn . As Kast had become prominent during the Galactic Civil War, Skoll became aware of his existence, and kept his name, alongside the names of other Coalition heroes, listed in a device on his gauntlet during the search for the planet Peridea in a distant stellar realm Recruiting his nephew He's an amazing teacher. Very strong. I've learned so much from him… ―Ben Corbin, on his uncle Though reluctant to take on another apprentice at first, when the Synth Flux awakened in Kast's nephew Ben Corbin and his mother Mira could see the deep flux taking hold of her son, she entrusted the boy to her brother to help him find balance.
He agreed to train the 10-year-old Corbin, in 15 ABY Kast took on the responsibilities of a Keeper Master by teaching his nephew. After Ben's fall to the deep flux however, Joren would say that he was merely playing the role of Keeper Master as it was a title he had not formally earned.
Kast taught Corbin the ways of the Keepers, in its many forms, under the tutelage and impressed on his pupil the importance of practicing discipline and control. Corbin gave his best efforts to gain his Master's approval, although at times their familial bond caused him to address Kast as uncle instead of Master.
Though he loved his nephew,
Kast had committed his life to reestablishing the Keepers of the Flux's teachings, which had virtually nothing to do with family ties; therefore, he taught Corbin that detachment and distance were necessary to gaining the pure focus that every Keeper aspired to achieve.
His nephew soon joined in exploring the stellar realm for ancient Keeper lore, and together they found holocrons from forgotten eras and ancient Keeper texts with arcane writings.
The two shared more than the same Kast blood, for like him, Ben gained a strong devotion to the ways of the Keepers of the Flux.
Kast saw his nephew's raw power and believed he could pass on his strengths to him, and he attempted to manage his progress through the Synth Flux by having him exercise a level of restraint.
Corbin was his strongest and most prized student, and Kast went on to place his hope for the Keepers of the Flux's future within his nephew, and despite knowing of the darkness within his pupil, the Keepers Master believed he was strong enough to keep it in check.
Kast was a kind master, and Corbin considered his uncle to be a strong and amazing teacher, but Kast made mistakes that culminated over time, protecting Ben from the dark truths of their family, much like his sister did before him, and inadvertently isolated Ben over the years of his training.
At the request of his sister, Joren never provided his nephew with the details of Xarn Velgor's fall to the deep flux or Xarn Velgor's redemption and return to the light.
During his training of his nephew, a powerful deep flux-practitioner known as Snoke who was the Supreme Leader of a splinter group of the Dominion known as the Nexus Command , and also secretly a puppet of the resurrected Overlord Draven Nul, began influencing Ben from afar, clouding him with the deep flux.
At some point Kast had encountered Snoke and supposedly damaged him, and Snoke was responsible for indirectly inspiring Joren to rebuild the Keepers of the Flux, having tried to shatter his resolve.
More apprentices We all studied together. I was first, but they came not long after. ―Ben Corbin, on Joren's training other apprentices Around 16 ABY Kast gathered disciples, young force-sensitive beings adrift with no way to harness their abilities as a result the Keepers Purge, who went on to be his first students.
Kast's new students numbered about a dozen besides his nephew, including students Voe Tai , and Hennix and he worked to teach them everything he had learned in his own training and about the bright flux of the Synth per Vorn's command.
Kast hoped that teaching the students in a real Keeper Temple would offer them a more traditional Keeper path than his own training under Zeth Malkor and Vorn. He kept temple's location on Ossus a secret known only to the members of the Order and his loyal automaton DR-7X.
Kast stored the recovered Keeper knowledge and relics that he and Lor San Tekka had recovered within the Keepers Temple, a decision which Corbin disagreed, which he believed was just locked up and never used.
With the small gathering of his new students looking to him as a spiritual guide and mentor for the Keepers arts, Kast believed he could usher in a new era of enlightenment.
With his attention now split between several other apprentices, Kast's nephew Ben Corbin's focus was drawn away to dark Flux knowledge.
Teaching several Keeper techniques and traditions as telekinesis, meditation, and the use and construction of flux blades Kast's approach for guiding the young minds under his care was steeped in his own master Vorn's teachings passed on while he was on Myrkon, so would often be preaching about patience and balance.
While also instilling the belief that all beings were equal within the Synth Flux, he gave lectures outside his temple, passing on ever more experience from training with Vorn on Myrkon as he taught the levitation of rocks through the Synth Flux. During one such event, student Voe asked why Ben Corbin was stronger, where Kast compared the Synth Flux to a door, that it would become easier to use the more she opened herself up to it. The Keeper Master also tutored Hennix in opening holocrons through the Synth Flux, reminding the young Quarren to listen to the unique voices of each holocron, for each one was different based on their teachings.
Kast desired heavily to preserve the teachings of the Keepers of the Flux, laboring to pass on the best of what Vorn and Zeth taught him as Keeper mentors at his Keeper temple and to instill the core beliefs they embodied within the next generation. Additionally, Joren understood what Vorn experienced with himself on Myrkon as teacher, having the same unique frustrations with his own pupils; a master guiding a young unseasoned student that was convinced they were wasting time on pointless exercises, such as stacking stones.
At some point during his time as a Keeper Master, Kast would face a group of IG-RM assassin automatons alongside his nephew.
Encountering the Knights of Ren Well, hello there. Everything here is ours. We're going to take it…but how we take it is up to you. Your call, friends.
You're wrong. So, so wrong. This is a Keeper outpost . Everything in it belongs to the Keepers of the Flux, which, at the moment, means me. I am the last Keeper. ―Ren and Kast In 19 ABY , the year his nephew turned fourteen,
Kast, Ben, and Lor San Tekka traveled to a Keeper outpost on Elphrona , a location from the days of the High Concordium Era, hoping to find Keeper artifacts. Joren flew the ship and conversed with San Tekka, while Corbin, without his master's knowledge, was communing with Snoke. Kast was initially skeptical of a Keeper presence so far from the core, but San Tekka assured him that with his expertise he had recognized the description from his contact as a Keeper Outpost, and explained that the Keepers's activity was greatly expanded throughout the stellar realm during the High Concordium Era. Meanwhile, Corbin telepathically communicated with Snoke, expressing that he was tired of missions to find artifacts with Kast that he would just lock away in the temple and never use. Corbin admitted that Kast was an amazing teacher, but was frustrated that he never wanted Corbin to use any of the things he had taught him, which Snoke claimed was because Kast viewed Corbin as a child and had not realized his true capabilities. Kast then told Corbin that they would possibly find insights into the old Keeper, and added that they could find interesting weapons, as he knew that Corbin was interested in such subjects, which San Tekka affirmed. Kast told Corbin he was glad he came along, and Corbin said he shared the sentiment.
When they landed, Kast was impressed by the sight of the outpost. He cautioned his apprentice to careful and warned that such a place was likely to be rigged with traps, recalling a time he found a flux blade in a similar place that turned out to be a trap and nearly killed him. Inside, they found a treasure trove that Kast judged so impressive he thought it may be better than Jocasta Nu's cache, including many Keeper holocrons and a flux blade rifle . Kast was excited by the possibility of learning many new things. As the three perused the outpost, instantly, both Kast and Corbin felt a cold presence within the structure. They were then confronted by a group of deep flux warriors known as the Knights of Ren revealed themselves.
Their master, "
Ren ," declared themselves, and claimed ownership of all within the outpost and that they way they took it was up to Kast. Kast countered, shooting back that all within the outpost, due it being a Keeper location, belonged to the Order; all the holocrons and relics belonged to Joren, for he was the last Keeper Knight even though he would not be for long. Kast told the Knights he could sense the deep flux in them and would never let them have the artifacts. When Ren insulted him, Corbin stepped forward and declared that his master was a legend. Kast ignited his saber and instructed San Tekka to hang back and Corbin to protect San Tekka, while handled the threat, assuring Corbin he could do it even thought there were seven of them. Ren tried to taunt him, but Kast just taunted him back, angering him and moving him to order the Knights to attack. As Corbin watched on and called out to him, Kast engaged the knights, not intending to kill any of them, and managed to overpower them quite easily despite their numbers, which he explained as him not being alone because he had the powerful ally that was the Synth Flux by his side. He criticized them for their crude use of the Synth Flux, saying they wielded the deep flux like a hammer, contrasted by his blade-like precision with the bright flux. He eventually used the Synth Flux to destroy the Knights' weapons and proceeded to prepare himself for a duel with Ren , who wielded a red flux blade . Ren informed Kast that he activated a kill switch on his flux blade that would detonate the room if it left his hand. Kast warned that move might not go the way Ren expected, which Ren agreed with. Realizing that Kast's power was far superior to their own, Ren withdrew his knights and left the planet. However, before he left, Ren removed his helmet and warned Kast about the strong presence of the deep flux within Corbin, before offering the boy chance, if he chose, to seek them out if decided he wanted another path.
Ultimately, the structure proved to be a source of more knowledge for Kast, for he recovered the holocrons and other relics inside, leaving the outpost empty, but his nephew was prompted with the deep flux of the Synth.
Quest for Exegol Visions and voices But this…place, whatever it is. I'm seeing it for a reason, Lor. I can feel it. ―Joren Kast to Lor San Tekka on his visions As his first disciples grew older and new younglings came into the New Keepers of the Flux's care, Kast had a Twi'lek named Enyo help him as one of the younglings' instructors.
In 21 ABY Kast started feeling an increasing darkness in the Synth Flux and having dark visions that frequently came to him in meditation. One day, Lor San Tekka, who was the only person Kast told about the visions, visited Kast in his temple on Ossus, his old friend's arrival heralded to Kast by Ben Corbin. San Tekka greeted Kast and Corbin, but once the latter was gone, asked Kast about the visions and whether or not they had become stronger or changed. Kast asked San Tekka to help him understand the visions and learn what the place in them was by standing vigil over him while he accessed the vision and described it. San Tekka was hesitant, but agreed, and Kast thus began the meditation.
Much quicker than his other meditations, Kast found himself in an empty dark void but felt an all-consuming presence within it which he had only thought he had sensed before. Then the vision shifted and he found himself standing on the ancient hidden Shade Covenant world of Exegol , though he did not know that was what he was seeing. Eventually the vision broke, and Kast explained to San Tekka what he had seen. San Tekka wondered if the vision was just a general representation of lingering darkness or an influence from an artifact, but Kast still believed he was having the vision for a reason. Kast asked San Tekka the reason he had come to Ossus in the first place, and he explained that there was an archaeological dig on the planet Yoturba that he wanted to check out with Kast, convincing him to leave the temple by saying that it would be good for Corbin to learn responsibilities while he was gone.
Yoturba I saw… a place. Darkness, and lightning. It was so dry , so old. And…There was something there. A…person. No. A shape. Like a…a shadow. It was…on me, I could feel it, I could hear it speaking to me. ―Mico Haswell to Joren Kast, after being influenced by a void crystal bled with the deep flux of the Synth Kast and San Tekka arrived at the archeological expedition on Yorturba that was being conducted by the Lerct Historical Institute and were greeted by archaeologist Dr.
Beaumont Kin , who informed them that the dig site was disappointing and they had not found a Keeper temple. Kast still carried a feeling that he was there for a reason despite this, and he would be proven correct when an archaeologist named Tee Mairi discovered a Shade Covenant holocron at the site. Kast recognized it immediately, and San Tekka recognized the writing on it as the Shade Covenant's mirrored Aurebesh . Kast still was not convinced that it was what he had sensed there, and would be again proved correct when he heard screaming echoing throughout the site. Archeologist Mico Haswell had discovered a Shade Covenant void crystal and had his mind influenced by it, causing him to uncontrollably lash out and cause destruction with the Synth Flux. Joren restrained him with the Synth Flux, but in reaching out to him once again had a vision of Exegol that Haswell was now present in. Haswell leapt toward Kast in the vison and moved in a way that Kast recognized to be inhuman and even reptilian. Then Kast was on Yoturba again and managed to restrain the man with the Synth Flux again, calling a team of medics to sedate him via an injection.
Haswell was once again in control of himself, and Kast questioned him about what happened, though the man was not able to remember much. He did tell Kast that he saw a dry, dark place filled with lightning and felt a shadow-like sentient presence there. Once Haswell passed out, Kast discovered the bled void crystal in his hand, and confiscated it. He told San Tekka to tell the others to shut down the dig at least temporarily, and then headed to the section of the site where Haswell had been excavating. He discovered that the chamber was actually a crashed ship, and discovered more bled void crystals within it, that like the one Haswell had been holding, were the wrong size and shape for a flux blade. This and his experience on the planet confirmed for Kast that something dark and evil was coming, deciding to leave San Tekka there to look over the dig site while he went to Tython in search of answers.
Ghosts of the past Remember, my son, no matter how dark the night may grow, you are never alone. You will know what to do, my son. Let the Synth Flux guide you. Let it flow through you as it flows through me. Use that power, and your instincts, for they are one and the same. ―Xarn Velgor, to Joren Kast Kast traveled to Tython and visited the ruined Keeper temple known as the Synth Flux-henge, where he sat on the seeing stone and began to study the Shade Covenant Holocron and the void crystals and perform research through his many ancient tomes and personal notes. In a book that was a catalog of the Eeshaypher Collection, Kast discovered an entry about a Shade Covenant wayfinder , which he found remarkably similar in appearance and utility to the Shade Covenant holocron. The book stated that the wayfinders contained navigational data to an ancient Shade Covenant world called Ixigul—or in Kast's translation: Exegol. Kast gleaned from both the catalog and his other research that Exegol was meant to be a place of power and eternal life, and that its secret was so important that only two Shade Covenant wayfinders were ever made. He theorized that the crashed ship from Yoturba had been from someone attempting to use the holocron and the void crystals to navigate to Exegol, and thus decided to use the power of the seeing stone to meditate while reaching out to the crystals and holocron to see what he could discover.
During his meditation, Kast once again had a vision of Exegol, but this time if felt like he was truly on the planet, causing him to wonder if the information stored in the holocron had allowed the seeing stone to actually transport him to the planet. Kast was then approached by nine Shade Covenant wraiths on the planet who ignited red flux blades and began to attack him. Kast could block their flux blades with his own, but could not attack the wraiths themselves, his blade and his body merely passing right through them. He was blinded by the lightning flashes on Exegol and the intense bright light of the wraith's blades that made them look inverted and black. Kast started struggling to fight them more and more until all nine wraiths eventually went in for one coordinated strike, but before they could a blue glow in the shape of a person holding a flux blade appeared in from of Kast and pushed them away. Kast realized he was being assisted by a Flux-spirit with a blue-bladed flux blade and initially thought was Malkor, but when the figure lowered his hood, Kast learned that it was his father, Xarn Velgor. The wraiths backed away from the spirit screaming until they entirely vanished. Xarn Velgor then reached out his hand to Joren, who took it and then found himself back on Tython as if he had never left.
Joren found that many of his books were burning and the holocron was completely destroyed, putting them out before speaking to Anakin. Anakin's figure flashed in and out and shifted between him as a young Keeper and him as the old man Joren had seen behind the mask of Xarn Velgor. Joren noticed that he seemed to be in pain and afraid. Anakin explained that there was a disturbance in the Synth Flux that was a shadow from an ancient time. He also clarified that the seeing stone had sent a part of Joren to Exegol and that it had taken everything in him to bring him back. Anakin reminded Joren that he could divert the path of the Synth Flux if he needed to, and that no matter how dark things seemed he was never alone as long as he allowed the Synth Flux to be his guide. Anakin then disappeared and left Joren alone until he heard the sound of a ship flying overhead and landing. It was Drev Kass, in the Lady Luck , who greeted Joren, but immediately told him that he needed to leave and that it was too dangerous for him to be there. But Drev Kass told him that Lor San Tekka had told him where he was because Drev Kass had information about the Shade Covenant, and also believed they were back.
Drev Kass's mission We need to find the family. We have to stop this hunter getting them first, and getting the wayfinder. Joren, listen, you said it yourself. There's a disturbance in the Synth Flux, a, what did you call it, a shadow, and it's coming at us fast. ―Drev Kass, to Kast on the family being pursued by Ochi of Bestoon Drev Kass brought Kast onto his ship and explained that overheard an assassin named Ochi of Bestoon who had become inebriated at a remote bar bragging that he had been sent by a Shade Covenant remnant to kidnap a girl from her parents. Lando elaborated that he had checked out a New Concordium report, about Halo Squadron fighting off hunters attacking the family's ship Wild Space that backed up Ochi's story, explaining that the New Concordium had apparently relocated the family after the incident. He then displayed a hologram of a hex charm that the family had been carrying as proof that they were being hunted by the Shade Covenant. Kast recognized it immediately as a symbol of the Shade Covenant and decoded mirrored Aurebesh on it to be a Shade Covenant saying.
Drev Kass expressed his worry over Ochi's implication that the Shade Covenant were still out there pulling strings, and wondered why they would want that family and go through the trouble of kidnapping the girl. Kast told Drev Kass about his visions of Exegol, and told him that the way to the planet was hidden in a Shade Covenant wayfinder. Drev Kass added that he had heard Ochi mention that he would have a way to Exegol soon, and wondered if the family had a wayfinder or knew where one was. But unbeknownst to Kast and Drev Kass, the father of the family was a strand-cast of the late Overlord Draven Nul named Dathan , and the girl, his daughter named Rey , was thus Draven Nul's genetic granddaughter.
Drev Kass decided that he and Kast needed to go together to find the family and stop the hunter from getting to them, urging Kast to get ahead of the shadow that he had sensed before it was too late. Kast was hesitant to bring Drev Kass along, but agreed to partially because he was aware of Drev Kass's daughter's kidnapping and his friend's desperate hope that investigating this kidnapping could lead to her. They agreed to meet at the Adelphi system where Drev Kass had a contact with the New Concordium that they hoped could tell them where the New Concordium had sent the family.
Nightside It's them. Those are the same kind of ships that were chasing the family. […] Okay, we're out of time. We have to get to that hangar.
The deep flux. It's here. I can feel it. ―Drev Kass and Kast, as Ochi's forces and the Shade Covenant arrive on Nightside Kast and Drev Kass met with the latter's contact,
Shriv Suurgav , in the New Concordium staging outpost over the planet Adelphi . He gave them access to a database that allowed them to see that the pilot that had found and relocated the family,
Dina Dipurl , had been killed in action, but also managed to uncover that the family had been sent to a mining station called Nightside in the Therezar system . Kast and Drev Kass traveled to the station with the hope of making contact with Drev Kass's old acquaintance Zargo Anaximander . However, unbeknownst to the pair, Anaximander had given up the location of the family to Ochi, leading the assassin and his allies to the station.
Using a clearance code that Anaximander had given Drev Kass years earlier, Kast and Drev Kass were able to land their ship in the VIP hangar of Nightside. They located the family's ship but discovered that it was empty. Using the Synth Flux, Kast located the family and sensed that they were aboard Anaximander's personal yacht Goldstone . DR-7X then tapped into the station's surveillance systems and discovered from recordings that Dathan and Miramir were planning to escape with their daughter Rey. Drev Kass then realized that his "old friend" Anaximander had actually been holding the family captive against their will.
Before Kast and Drev Kass can reach the family, the station under attack by the Droid Crush Pirates of Bestoon, who, even though they were working for Ochi, ignored Ochi's orders for a clean extraction of the family and instead performed a raid on Nightside . Kast was able to sense in the Synth Flux that the attackers were the same people pursuing the family. During the attack, Kast and Drev Kass split up, Kast tasking Drev Kass with helping the family escape while he took on the assailants.
Left alone, Kast used the Synth Flux to track a Shade Covenant presence that he felt, ending up in a cable train station. A masked Pantoran woman then landed there in a customized imperial TIE Defender , and when she exited it, Kast noticed that she appeared to be a Shade Covenant and was wielding the ancient lost flux blade of Darth Noctyss . The two began to duel, the woman ordering Kast to hand her the shards of the bled Kyber crystal from Yoturba. Realizing that she was possessed by a Shade Covenant spirit, Kast offered to help free her while warning that he would defend himself. Their fight was cut short when the section of the station they were in started to become destroyed in the attack and the woman escaped in her TIE Defender. Kast was saved by Drev Kass in the Lady Luck , who informed Kast that he had helped the family escape the station in Anaximander's personal Yacht the Goldstone Torrenoteer Minor I'm sorry, Lando. I'm sorry for everything. And of course it means the same to me as it does to you. That family needs us. We might be their only hope.
What's this, a Keeper with feelings? I thought that wasn't supposed to happen. Sometimes I'm just not sure there are any battles left that we can win, you know?
These people are important, and we're going to find them. ―Kast consoles Drev Kass on the loss of his daughter and resolves to find the fugitive family Kast and Drev Kass followed the Goldstone via transmissions aboard the Lady Luck , Kast telling Kast about his encounter with the Shade Covenant acolyte on Nightside. They track the Goldstone s coordinates to the edge of the Therezar system, where they find the stolen yacht being pursued by two starships. Before the Goldstone jumped into voidspace, DR-7X was able to deduce that the family was fleeing to the Torrenoteer system . Following the trail of the Goldstone , Kast and Drev Kass land Lady Luck on the desert world of Torrenoteer Minor , the two continuing their search for the family on two Stacker Polaris Stormwolf speeder bikes . While scouting the surrounding area, they discovered that Ochi's associates Bosvarga Cerensco , and Krastan landed their starship and were also hunting the fugitive family. When the bounty hunters split into two parties, Kast and Drev Kass also decided to split up in order to track the hunters.
While riding his speeder bike through a ravine system, Kast was ambushed by the two bounty hunters, also riding speeder bikes. Kast deflected their pulse rifle bolts of using his flux blade, and hit one bolt back at one of the hunters, causing the hunter's speeder bike to crash in a ravine. Kast chased the second speeder over a lake, once again deflecting the hunter's pulse rifle bolts, causing the hunter and his speeder bike to fall in the lake by hitting the bolts back at him. Kast then approached the Weequay hunter in his wrecked vehicle and attempted to question him, but he fought Kast instead and subsequently died from being impaled by his speeder bike's steering vane. While examining the hunter's bike, Kast discovered a message indicating that the hunter's associates believe they have found something "nice and juicy."
Heading to coordinates transmitted by the hunter's associates, Kast found that the Gran hunter Krastan had captured Lando and was holding him in the prisoner clamp of a CAP-2 walker. Kast fought Krastan with his flux blade, killing him by defecting his own pulse rifle bolts back at him. He then battled the CAP-2 walker's pilot while Drev Kass broke free of the prisoner clamp. Kast sliced the CAP-2's left leg with his flux blade, causing the machine to stumble, before using the Synth Flux to throw the machine to the ground. After taking out the pilot, Kast rejoined and attended to Lando, who explained that the hunters had followed him to the Goldstone but that the ship had fled before they could breach it. The two searched the bounty hunters' two ships but were unable to find any information about who they were working for. While attempting to access one of the ship's navigation computers, Kast inadvertently activated a self-destruct mechanism that destroyed the ship's systems, preventing them from tracking down the family's ship. Drev Kass then revealed that the mission was reigniting his frustration about not being able to find his lost daughter.
Polaar Yupe Tashu was revealed as the true master of the Acolytes of the Beyond. […] He carried many such artifacts of the old times with him, including weapons and void crystals. But most important of all was the mask of Viceroy Exim Panshard. It was to Kiza he bequeathed this most powerful of relics.
She put it on, and Exim Panshard lived again. ―Komat and Drev Kass, on how Kiza became possessed by Shade Covenant lord Exim Panshard Kast had begun to suspect that the woman he encountered was related to a deep flux cult called the Acolytes of the Beyond that he had fought before. Kast and Drev Kass used his friend Lina Graf 's ship the Star Herald to travel to the planet Polaar to get information from Komat , a woman who was formerly part of the Acolytes of the Beyond who collected Shade Covenant artifacts like the flux blade of Darth Noctyss . Kast also believed that she would have the ability to track the Goldstone for them. When Kast had previously encountered the Acolytes of the Beyond, he had met her and had helped her leave the cult. When they arrived on Polaar, a radioactive planet cut off from outside communication by an impenetrable interference field, they were greeted by a former Dominion mouse automaton called M-1 and a sword-wielding humanoid automaton named KB-68 , who was serving as Komat 's sentry. Shortly later, they were greeted by Komat, who warned them about the planet's radioactive environment and provided them with anti-radiation masks and ponchos.
Agreeing to help them if they helped her in turn, Komat took them to a wrecked Mon Calamari cruiser that crashed on Polaar during the Galactic Civil War, explaining that with the shields overloaded, the ship's fusion core had melted down, contaminating the planet with distronic radiation that rendered it uninhabitable for most lifeforms. Kast and Drev Kass help Komat repair her particle capture grid, which she used to harvest radiation that KB-68 sold for supplies in the Pirsen system . In return, Komat revealed that the woman Kast had encountered was named Kiza , and had once been her friend. She explained that Kiza had worked directly for Yupe Tashu , the leader Acolytes of the Beyond and one of the Overlord's advisors, who had gifted her the Mask of Viceroy Exim Panshard which contained the preserved consciousness of the ancient Shade Covenant lord Exim Panshard and allowed him to take over her when she wore it. Kast revealed that they believed she was working with Ochi to hunt down a fugitive family in order to obtain a wayfinder. Explaining that two Shade Covenant wayfinders were made as navigational guides to Exegol, the hidden Shade Covenant world, Komat became determined to help Kast and Drev Kass stop Kiza and keep her from reaching Exegol.
Komat was able to locate the Goldstone using hyperwave signal interception technology, but shortly after she did automatons sent by Kiza attacked the planet . Kast and Drev Kass join Komat, KB-68, and Komat's feline pet targon Sekhmet in battling the automatons. Kast and the others managed to defeat them, and after being brought one of the automaton's heads by M-1, they discovered that the automatons contained bled void crystals that were also pieces of the large broken one Kast had found on Yoturba. Kast decided that he would use the automatons' navigational data to locate Kiza and make sure that she and subsequently Exim Panshard did not make it to Exegol, and thus make sure the Shade Covenant did not return. He did this with the assurance that while he did Drev Kass and Komat would head to Taw Provode , where they had tracked the family to, and try to save the family from their pursuers.
Kiza I can help you right now, Kiza. I can see what the mask has done to you. How it feeds off you, off your anger and your pain. The more you suffer, the stronger it gets. It will kill you, and then when you die, it will find someone else. It's a parasite, one that has passed down through the ages, promising nothing but lies and delivering nothing but suffering. ―Kast to Kiza, on the mask of Shade Covenant lord Exim Panshard Using coordinates retrieved from the automaton lander's navigation computer , Kast and DR-7X located Kiza by tracking the attackers' origin to an asteroid at the edge of the Red Honeycomb Zone . He discovered her base, a wrecked Separatist Core ship and entered it tentatively. He discovered a automaton forge and a meditation chamber with the Mask of Exim Panshard sitting on a plinth inside, but when he looked at it was overwhelmed with all consuming vision's of Panshard's life, including his acts of mass murder committed against his people in order to drain his life force in an attempt to live forever. When Kiza arrived, Kast attempted to try and appeal to her and choose a good path, with the woman even clearly expressing regret. He insisted that the mask was using her and she did not have to listen to it, but Kiza explained that it had promised her rebirth on Exegol, which Kast told her was a lie. His warnings that the deep flux only leads to suffering and death fell on deaf ears as she was coerced by the mask to put it back on.
Kiza engaged Kast in flux blade combat, fueled by the knowledge of Panshard. Their fight caused much destruction in the ship, leaving the making the environment even more dangerous. While fighting, Kiza used the Synth Flux in attempt to pour hot molten metal onto Kast, but the Keepers Master used the Synth Flux to escape the ensuing molten fire in the automaton foundry. However, molten material began to spread everywhere melt the ship. Kast attempted one last time to reach Kiza, pleading with her to leave it behind and join him. Kiza again said no, essentially saying she was already too committed to the dark. The core ship collapsed and rubble fell on top of and killed Kiza, much to Kast's dismay.
Kast then retrieved several ancient texts from the meditation chamber but was unable to find Panshard's mask. He returned to the Star Herald and discovered a holomessage from Drev Kass asking for him to go to Taw Provode as quick as he could to help with their situation with the family and Ochi of Bestoon there. But unbeknownst to Kast, Panshard's mask survived, and as he departed some of Kiza's automatons followed programming to reforge the mask with melted bled void crystals, before placing the mask on Kiza's dead body, allowing Panshard to live again by possessing and reanimating her body.
Clue to Exegol Joren and I were tailing an old Keeper hunter. Ochi of Bestoon. He was carrying a clue that could lead to a wayfinder. We followed his ship halfway across the stellar realm here. When we got to his ship, it was abandoned.... No clue. No wayfinder. ―Drev Kass, on his and Joren's mission to Ardath Kast arrived on Taw Provode just as Drev Kass and Komat had been discovered and captured by Ochi of Bestoon and his subordinate Corsec forces. Kast faced off with Ochi, and was surprised that his flux blade did not cut through Ochi's dagger. The power of Ochi's dagger allowed him to hold himself off against the Keepers for some time until Kast managed to use his flux blade to ignite a fuels reserve and trigger a massive explosion. Panshard appeared in his TIE Defender and started shooting at Kast and the others, leading Kast to realize that the mask was not really destroyed and the ancient Shade Lord lived on. Kast and Komat decided to chase him into space, while Drev Kass and DR-7X went after the family.
Kast and Komat followed Panshard to Taw Provode's solitary moon , using the Star Herald to shoot his TIE Defender down and strand him on the moon. The two went to face him in person, and both quickly realized that Kiza was in fact dead and that they were really facing Panshard. Panshard told Kast to tell him how to get to Exegol or be killed, but Kast and Komat refused and instead faced off with their flux blades. Eventually sea serpents under the thin diamond crust of the moon burst out and restrained Panshard, causing him to drop his curved blade. Kast picked it up and then called the mask to him with the Synth Flux before slicing it into four pieces with the two flux blades and destroying Panshard for good.
Kast was informed by Drev Kass that he had found the bodies of the parents of the family, but that the girl was still missing. Kast, Drev Kass, DR-7X, and Komat traveled to Neftali , where they buried them and honored their lives. Kast and Drev Kass worried that Ochi had successfully captured the girl and taken her to Exegol, but they could not be sure, and decided to keep looking for Ochi.
After Drev Kass took a time to recover by working with Komat at her homestead, Kast and Drev Kass eventually tracked Ochi to Ardath where they found his ship but no Ochi. They did find a sheet of old-looking paper with a wayfinder drawn on it that proved that Ochi had had a clue to a wayfinder, but other than that, the trail went cold.
Kast then returned to Ossus to continue running his Keeper academy and left Drev Kass on Pasanna, who decided to use the planet as his base to look for both the lost girl and his daughter. Kast stored the pieces of bled void crystal and the shards of Exim Panshard's mask in the temple with other artifacts.
Kast would chronicle his efforts to trace the disturbance in the Synth Flux and find the Shade Covenant Wayfinder in the Rammahgon throughout his explorations in the stellar realm.
Telling a story Considering… Well, your family, your skill in the Synth Flux, and that you'll be one of the first students to complete your training… It seems likely that you could hold a position of leadership in the Keepers of the Flux to come. ―Joren Kast, to Ben Corbin During Ben's training on Ossus, Kast asked his nephew to accompany him on a walk through the temple grounds. As they walked, Kast told Corbin that he was almost ready to undergo the Keepers Trials , the completion of which would mark the end of their time together as Master and apprentice.
According to Kast, Corbin would no longer have to listen to a master since he would be free to make his own decisions as a fully-trained Keeper Knight. In addition, Kast acknowledged Corbin's potential to become a future leader in the Keepers of the Flux that he was working to restore, given his nephew's status as a Kast, his strength in the Synth Flux, and the fact that he stood poised to be among the first in his generation to complete his instruction in the Keepers arts. Having faced his own challenges as a leader, Kast wished to tell his apprentice a story about an incident from his past, its purpose to serve as a lesson in making difficult decisions.
Kast's story centered on his discovery and subsequent use of the Grim Rose , an ancient weapon that he discovered upon returning to the Living Sea on the planet Gazian . Corbin learned that Kast had considered using the Grim Rose to assassinate Overlord Draven Nul; using a sample of Draven Nul's blood, the weapon could be used to target and kill the Overlord. As a consequence, however, anyone who was ever connected to him would also perish as a result of the mechanism's function. Corbin theorized that the weapon had the capacity to destroy the Shade Covenant. Being aware of Velgor's connection to Draven Nul, Corbin was certain that the weapon would have scanned Velgor as well, ultimately resulting in the deaths of both Shade Lords. Kast revealed that while he had considered that possibility, he was still reluctant to allow the Grim Rose to carry out its programming, having begun to think that he could redeem Velgor. By the end of the story, Kast revealed that the lesson behind the story was "to not defeat evil by becoming evil." Corbin questioned if it was a true story or simply a parable, to which Kast assured him that it was all true.
Fallen apprentice Ben Corbin's betrayal I saw darkness. I sensed it building in him. I'd seen it in moments during his training. But then I looked inside, and it was beyond what I ever imagined. Snoke had already turned his heart. He would bring destruction, pain, death, and the end of everything I love because of what he will become. And for the briefest moment of pure instinct, I thought I could stop it. It passed like a fleeting shadow. And I was left with shame and with consequence. And the last thing I saw were the eyes of a frightened boy whose master had failed him. ―Joren Kast One night in 28 ABY Kast, sensing the darkness within his nephew Ben growing in glimpses during his training, decided to probe Corbin's mind as he lay asleep. He learned that Snoke had turned his apprentice's heart to the deep flux, and experienced a brief dark vision that Corbin would bring nothing but death and destruction and leave Kast's loved ones in anguish. Despite the love for his nephew, in a flash of defensive instinct,
Kast thought he could stop that future and for a brief moment of pure instinct he raised his flux blade to destroy him, believing that it was the only way to halt his descent into darkness and prevent the end of everything he loved. This instinct was gone almost the second that it had come, and Kast was immediately filled with shame.
Kast had failed to correctly view that his nephew's mind was still conflicted, and his choice not yet made between light and darkness.
From Rey's point of view, Kast's failure as not only Corbin's Keeper Master, but his uncle and family, had been completed when this occurred.
Corbin had already awoken to see his uncle standing over him with his flux blade raised. Confused and angry, Corbin pulled his own flux blade to him in self-defense and attacked Kast, using the Synth Flux to pull down the hut on top of him.
Corbin emerged from the hut and screamed toward the temple, asking Kast why he had done it, and in his anger bringing down a lightning strike with the Synth Flux that destroyed the temple and set it on fire.
When Kast came around, he emerged from the rubble of the hut to find his temple destroyed, with his other apprentices either killed or vanished along with Corbin. This effectively left Kast the last of the Keepers once more as he was after the death of his own master, Vorn, decades earlier.
Three of Kast's apprentices, Tai, Hennix, and Voe, eventually returned to the temple while it burned and were not able to sense his presence, which they normally felt overwhelmingly in the Synth Flux. When Corbin claimed that Kast had tried to kill him and that he was dead, they did not believe the former, but did believe the latter because they could not sense their master's presence. Corbin claimed that he was stronger than Kast when it mattered and left the planet, the three pursuing Corbin with the hope of avenging Kast. When Corbin considered leaving to go to his mother in the aftermath, he heard the voice of Snoke in his head perpetuating his belief that Kast had tried to kill him, as he and Corbin had feared it before, claiming that Kast was "small" and feared Corbin and his strength. Snoke continued to speak to Corbin, blaming Kast for everything that had happened and claiming he chose it, and Corbin decided to go to Snoke instead of his mother. On the Amaxine Space Station , with Snoke, Corbin noticed the man's appearance and asked him what Kast had done to him, but Snoke redirected the conversation to what Kast had done to Corbin. Corbin tried to claim it did not matter because he had killed the Keepers Master, but Snoke warned that Kast was not dead and would not die so easily, though agreed to stop talking about Kast because he had "stolen" too much of his life already.
Corbin eventually became the apprentice of Snoke and took the new name "Kylo Ren," becoming the leader of a group of deep flux warriors known as the Knights of Ren.
Left deeply affected and mentally broken by what he believed to be his ultimate failure, Kast lost faith in the Synth Flux and the vision of the Keepers for having failed to rebuild the Keepers of the Flux. Kast believed he had failed to protect his students, and also failed to save his own flesh and blood from his family's dark legacy. So Kast fled into self-imposed exile, vanishing from the stellar realm at-large without a trace. No one knew for sure where he went, but those closest to him believed he went off in search of the first Keeper Temple.
To those who knew what Kast was seeking, there remained a way to find him: after destroying the Keepers, the Dominion had begun to seek out the thousands of Keeper temples scattered across the stellar realm, with the aim to prevent anyone from discovering the lost secrets of the Order. As such, Dominion authorities discovered the locations of some of the first Keeper Temples. The partial map remained within the archives of the Dominion. The Nexus Command was able to acquire the partial map from the archives, that was missing a fraction owned by Lor San Tekka.
As he had downloaded Dominion star maps during his time on the first Void Core,
DR-7X, Kast's faithful astromech, was able to recover the contents of the map.
Kylo Ren's vision Your Uncle feared you. Feared how formidable you could become. Your hatred for him is right. Kast would have murdered you in your sleep. Rise above what's holding you back. Strike. Strike against the bright flux within you! STRIKE! ―Snoke to Kylo Ren as he fought a vision of Kast Later, while training Kylo Ren with an exercise in which he forced Ren to stop himself from falling, Snoke informed the boy that Kast would have caught him if he had not caught himself, and would have thus held him back. Snoke then took Ren to the Cave of Evil on Myrkon, where Ren could sense that Kast had been there, Snoke revealing that he had trained there before becoming one of the most formidable Keeper. Ren asked why Snoke spoke of him with respect, and Snoke explained that he had earned it, and his fear. Ren tried to claim that Kast was weak, but Snoke refuted this, insisting that Kast was only misguided and that he would have taken over the stellar realm a long time before if Kast had been his apprentice.
Ren then entered the cave and found himself facing an apparition of Kast, who insisted that he did not want to fight. Ren fought him anyway, his red crossguard flux blade clashing against Kast's green. Snoke egged Ren on and validated his hatred as he fought his old master, insisting that Kast had feared what Ren would become, would have murdered him in his sleep, and was representative of the bright flux holding him back. Screaming as Snoke told him to strike, Ren sliced through Kast's body, leaving the appearance of his dead body lying on the ground of the cave. While staring at the corpse, Ren declared himself ready.
Exile on Ahch-To Shutting himself off from the Synth Flux You've closed yourself off from the Synth Flux. ―Rey, to Joren Kast The Keeper Master fled to the location of the Temple—on an aquatic planet named Ahch-To—that he discovered by studying the spread of uneti saplings.
Having failed in bringing back the Keepers of the Flux, Kast intended nothing more than to live out the rest of his days there in reclusive exile and had pledged to protect the hidden world and its many secrets.
Taking the relics he had uncovered with him, the hermit shut himself off from the Synth Flux consequently ceasing the guidance of Zeth Malkor, Vorn, and Xarn Velgor's spirits.
It had taken years for him to learn the discipline of blocking the Synth Flux out, and he considered the fact that it was harder to do so than to feel it and let it in a dark secret that was never taught. He believed the Synth Flux and the Keepers had many secrets and that not even the greatest and in his opinion arrogant Keeper Masters could understand all of it. Even though occasionally his greatest efforts would not be enough and the Synth Flux would reach him anyway, he felt as though he had achieved true solitude in closing himself off.
Among the relics Joren had collected and brought into his exile were the sacred Keeper texts and the Keepers Crusader pendant.
Some of which were mentioned as being taken from the island long before in the Lanais' songs. The Caretakers tolerated Kast's presence, though he had learned the Lanai's language and ways, insisted on doing most of their duties to care for him himself, and had appeared each month on the Festival of Return , something which puzzled Alcida-Auka Though absent from galactic affairs, Kast's story was nonetheless a thing of myth and legend in certain corners of the stellar realm. His exploits in the Galactic Civil War were known to the Resistance , a band of freedom fighters led by now-General Venn to combat the rise of the Nexus Command. Resistance fighter pilot Jessika Pava learned that LQ-9M, who also served in the Resistance, knew Kast and asked the automaton to tell her a story about his former master. Loquim regaled her with the tale of Kast's encounter with Sarco Plank on Devaron, and how Kast kept his promise to Farnay and indeed returned to Devaron.
Members of the Nexus Command, however, had a less-than-ideal view of the Keepers Master; the Nexus Command taught their followers, including legion vanguard like FN-2187 , that Kast was an enemy who helped destroy the Dominion. Despite the knowledge about Kast, the mystical elements of his life, as well as the Keepers in general, were seen as mythic rather than historic.
Prior to Rey's arrival on Ahch-To, Joren Kast had a dream while sleeping in his hut. In his dream, he saw the life he would never have, one in which he never left Vorath Prime and remained in the moisture farming business with his uncle, marrying Camie Marstrap and allowing the Solar Dominion to shatter the Free Worlds Coalition and destroy more planets with the Void Core. Joren eventually awoke, trying to shake away his dream, and left his hut. He was still astonished at the oceans of Ahch-To, which he attributed to growing up in the deserts of Vorath Prime. As he reached a meadow overlooking the sea, he was still unable to shake the dream away, believing it was not an ordinary dream, but the Synth Flux at work.
Also shortly before Rey arrived, one of the moments where the Synth Flux demanded to be heard and got through despite Kast's block occurred. In that moment, the old Keeper Master undeniably felt that the Synth Flux was telling him that someone had found him.
Kast is found You think what? I'm going to walk out with a laser sword and face down the whole Nexus Command? ―Joren Kast to Rey In 34 ABY , Venn sent Resistance fighter Reeve Janar, the son of Shara Bey and Kes Janar, to Vorath Minor to find San Tekka and recover the map to Kast's alleged location, as she hoped her brother would return and help in the fight against the deep flux. The Nexus Command also learned of the map and sought it so they could destroy the last Keeper. Janar found San Tekka living in the village of Tuanul , and the old explorer gave the map to Janar, who in turn gave it to his astromech automaton BB-8 , before Kylo Ren and the Nexus Command arrived and sacked the village. Ren killed San Tekka and took Janar into custody in the hopes of finding the map, while BB-8 came into the possession of a Vorath Minor scavenger named Rey. Rey believed that Kast was only a myth, but she too possessed strength in the Synth Flux that she had not yet discovered. Rey teamed with FN-2187, an ex-Legion Vanguard who took the name Finn after defecting from the Nexus Command, and, with the help of Dax Corbin and Gorath, sought to return the map fragment to the Resistance.
After Rey discovered the flux blade that once belonged to both Anakin and Joren in the pirate castle belonging to Maz Kanata on Takodana , a thousand year-old being who had knowledge of the Synth Flux, she began to unlock her own Flux abilities. After being captured by Ren on Takodana and briefly interrogated onboard Starkiller Base in an attempt by Ren to analyze Rey's thoughts to find the path to Kast, BB-8's map fragment was soon combined with DR-7X's, and together they provided a complete map to Ahch-To. Accompanied by Gorath, Rey flew the Shadow Hawk to Ahch-To and found the old Keeper Master on an island.
When Rey arrived on the island, Kast was standing up top the saddle , summoning the will to try to torch the tree library again having just failed to do so.
While standing there, Kast was reflecting that his efforts to block the Synth Flux from him was impossible as it always demanded to be heard. But in that moment, the old Keeper Master felt that someone had found him, turning around to see who was, discovering Rey.
After making her way to Kast, Rey presented him with his father's old weapon as a symbol of the only hope that the stellar realm had left.
Confronted with the past I can't teach you. […] I will never train another generation of Keeper. I came to this island to die. It's time for the Keepers to end. ―Kast, to Rey After taking his old flux blade and looking it over, Kast, emotion roiled over his face, hoping to lose the blade once again, tossed it behind him and left the plateau, with Rey following close behind him. Despite her repeated attempts, Kast declined to help her or the Resistance, even though he knew that the girl had been sent by his sister Mira.
Secluding himself in his hut, Kast thought to himself that he did not expect that the person who found him would be a child with what he considered a worthless antique and delusions of grandeur.
Later, Joren was folding his old clothes then heard Rey trying to reach out to him again by knocking on his door but he told her to go away. However, Joren was surprised when his door broke open but was even more surprised to see his old friend Gorath was with her. Hearing how they got to where he was with the Shadow Hawk, Joren asked where Dax was.
Rey and Gorath told him that his old friend and brother-in-law was dead, murdered by Kylo Ren, which stunned Kast. Feeling regret for not having sensed his brother-in-law's death because he had disconnected himself from the Synth Flux, Joren was comforted by the Thoran, who hugged him, before Kast comforted Gorath back.
While hiking the island with a backpack, Kast noticed the sacred uneti tree on the island and decided to go inside it. There he found Rey, who had discovered several original texts of Keeper teachings hidden there. Kast learned that she had the Synth Flux within her and sought assistance to understand it and her place in the stellar realm. Kast refused to teach her revealed that he had come to the place where the Keepers started in order to die, and voiced his belief that it was time for the Keepers of the Flux to die.
The next night, the former Keeper Master slipped into the Shadow Hawk and recalled the adventures he had experienced.
