While aboard, Kast reunited with R2 who, after he insisted that he could not be convinced to help, replayed Mira's holographic message to Malkor. Although Kast grumbled that the tactic "was a cheap move" on the automaton's part, it nevertheless inspired Kast to agree to give Rey some basic initiation training as a Keeper, explaining the basic foundations of the Synth Flux.
R2 also told Kast that she had grown up on a desert planet before discovered a automaton carrying secret data vital to the Coalition, which he recognized a similar story to his own. He approached Rey while she slept, telling her that R2 had told him her story and that he would give her three lessons to both teach her the ways of the Keepers and also show her why the Order needed to end.
A reluctant teacher and enlightenment What do you know about the Synth Flux?
It's a power that Keeper have that lets them control people… and make things float.
Impressive. Every word in that sentence was wrong. ―Joren Kast and Rey After hearing Rey's pulse rifle go off, which she claimed was because she was cleaning it, Kast gathered her for the first lesson and explained to her who the caretakers were. During the lesson, Kast explained to her that her idea of the Synth Flux as a power to have was wrong and that it was not about lifting rocks, explaining that it was an energy field throughout the universe. After Rey misunderstood Kast's instructions to reach out and reached out her arm, Kast pranked her by touching her hand with a leaf and telling her it was the Synth Flux before hitting her with it. Rey then tried again, Kast telling her that the Synth Flux and the light was so much more than just the Keepers as she felt it. However, she also sensed the presence of the deep flux cavern underneath the island, causing the ground to shake around them as Rey felt the pull of the deep flux.
Kast scolded her for going straight to the dark and not even trying to stop herself from taking what it was offering her. She also pointed out that she had not sensed him in the Synth Flux because he cut himself off. This moment made Kast realize that Rey's level of power was identical to that of his nephew Ben; this caused him to worry whether he should train another with such power and to promptly abandon the lesson.
But Kast did not just see Ben in her, he also saw much of himself, which he suspected was what scared him most.
Kast realized something else from this lesson; that Rey was the Overlord, Draven Nul', granddaughter, which is why she was so easily drawn to the deep flux.
He later found her practicing with a quarterstaff and then with the returned flux blade. Joren silently watched from afar, his concern and dismay increasing when he witnessed her ferocity with the weapon allowing her to slice through several rock formations apart with ease.
He recognized that she was focused and talented, but also that she was impetuous and driven by emotion, enmity, and impatience, which he knew lead to the deep flux and found all too familiar. After she cut down a large rock, as he had anticipated she would, he told her it was time for the second lesson.
Eventually, after the second lesson, Kast sat and spoke with Rey, telling her that the true legacy of the Keepers was failure, and revealing that they were responsible for the creation of Xarn Velgor. Rey pointed out that he had seen the light in Velgor and turned him, but Kast just wished that he had never told that story and thus not allowed it or himself to become a legend.
He then revealed that his nephew Ben had the same tremendous power in the Synth Flux she had and was corrupted by Snoke's teachings to the deep flux, becoming Kylo Ren. He then destroyed the Keepers temple Kast had started, taking some of his other apprentices with him and killing the rest. Rey ended the conversation by telling him that the stellar realm needed a legend, assuring him that he had not failed Ben and she would not fail him.
That night, Rey having reminded him that his solitude had cost him his connections to those he loved and not just his connection to the Synth Flux, Kast went to the meditation ledge . Realizing that he had abandoned his loved ones in selfish pity, he opened himself up to the Synth Flux.
He managed to regain his Flux abilities, allowing him to sense his sister from across the stellar realm.
Kast went to find Rey in order to tell her that he was going back to the Resistance with her but instead he found Rey talking with Kylo Ren through the Synth Flux due to a Flux-bond that he previously was unable to detect. Witnessing them touching hands,
Kast thought that they were in league. He had gestured at the walls and the ceiling of the hut Rey was in, and all the stone blocks were shot outward from the hut's center, scattering them around the bench.
Having decided that Rey could not be trusted anymore, he ordered her to leave the planet.
Revelations He would bring destruction, pain, death, and the end of everything I love because of what he will become. And for the briefest moment of pure instinct, I thought I could stop it. It passed like a fleeting shadow. And I was left with shame and with consequence. And the last thing I saw were the eyes of a frightened boy whose master had failed him. ―Joren Kast, about Ben Corbin, to Rey An enraged Rey proceeded to battle Kast with her staff, only to be swiftly disarmed after stumbling over a stone, leading to Rey threatening him with his father's flux blade. She revealed that, according to Kylo, Kast had tried to murder him while he was asleep and defenseless simply because he was afraid of his strength in the Synth Flux, sparking a duel between Uncle and Nephew. Kast clarified that when he had arrived at the hut, he sensed the power and darkness within his nephew and, for a brief instant of absolute pure instinct, he did intend to kill him and activated his flux blade.. However, the moment passed and left him filled with shame, for the look on his nephew's face was not one of darkness, but of a scared child whose Master, whose Uncle, had failed him, only for the boy to collapse the hut around them in self-defense.
Rey told Kast that like when he confronted his father about the bright flux still within him, she sensed the goodness still within Kylo Ren. Kast disagreed, saying that it is no longer there. Unable to convince him otherwise, Rey secretly stole the texts to teach herself later and left the planet without him to confront Kylo.
While Rey boarded the Shadow Hawk to leave, Kast saw R2 for the last time and told him to send his best to 3PO and that he would need to stay with Rey to make sure she got back to safety.
As Rey left Ahch-To, Joren realized the similarity between her departure and when Vorn and Malkor insisted him to remain on Myrkon to complete his training rather than abandon to save his friends at Cloud City.
Important lesson Joren, We are what they grow beyond. That is the true burden of all masters. ―Vorn The following night, Kast returned to the tree containing the Keepers texts with the intention of burning it to the ground. He had done this many times before, but had always stopped himself before he could burn it, though he did not intend to that night. As he approached the tree, his thoughts were answered by a voice, and when he turned he was greeted by the spirit of his old master Vorn, whom he had not seen in decades since he last saw him on Myrkon and Veldara.
Kast affirmed his desire to burn the tree with the texts inside.
Vorn informed him that he had no intentions of stopping him and reminded him that had never been able to prevent him from doing what he wanted.
Kast began to hesitate when he attempted to set the tree on fire. Vorn instead proceeded to summon a bolt of lightning, setting the tree alight and while laughing, repelled Kast with the flames when he changed his mind and tried to save the texts.
As the tree burned, the two masters sat next to each other and Vorn reminded Kast that he needed to heed his final instructions to him before Vorn's physical death on Myrkon, namely passing down what he had learned. Kast lamented over the loss of the texts, but Vorn slyly stated that although the texts held wisdom, 'page turners, they were not', and they did not contain anything Rey did not already have with her. Kast tried to explain that by failing Ben he felt that he had failed the Keepers. Vorn admonished Kast for still "constantly looking to the horizon," like he had when they met on Myrkon. Vorn explained that 'failure is the greatest teacher' and that failure was an essential part of what he had implored Kast to pass on so many years before.
Finding peace and purpose See you around, kid. ―Joren Kast's last words to Kylo Ren Kast later appeared at the Resistance base on Solara and greeted his sister for the first time in years, giving her Dax's gold dice to remember him by. Reconciling his failure to stop Ren and for not being there to prevent Dax's death, Kast resolved to finally confront Ren to buy the Resistance time to escape.
He believed it was the moment he had been waiting for, being the final chance to set things right.
Before Kast went to confront his nephew, he wordlessly greeted his old friend LQ-9M.
Stepping outside, Kast was spotted and targeted by a barrage of cannon fire from multiple AT-M6 walkers, but when the fusillade came to a stop, Kast strolled out of the smoke completely unharmed.
After Ren witnessed the impossible feat, Kast spoke to him in the Synth Flux, telling him that it wasn't over until it was just the two of them.
Ren landed on the surface and approached Kast alone and on foot. Ren taunted Kast, and mockingly asked him if he had come to save his former apprentice's soul. But Kast acknowledged that he was not there to redeem him.
Engaging his former master in combat, Ren charged at Kast but Kast simply danced around Ren without engaging in any form of counter attack. After evading Ren for a time, Kast stopped and apologized to Ren, stating that he had failed him. He also informed his former student that victory was not so certain as he believed; that the war was only beginning, and that the Keepers would continue through Rey. He then deactivated his flux blade and offered to let Kylo strike him down, but warned him that doing so in anger would only cause him pain, just as killing Dax had, and stated that in death, he would always be with his nephew.
Unknown to Kylo, Joren had sensed that Rey had lifted all the rocks blocking the exit of the Resistance base so they could escape to fight another day.
Ren ignored all these warnings and slashed Kast across the torso. Believing himself to have been victorious, he was instead astonished to find that his attack had had no effect on Kast. He walked over to his former master and tentatively ran him through, and discovered that Joren was not physically present; he was in fact still on Ahch-To, projecting an illusion of himself through the Synth Flux.
He thought to himself that was saving his best trick for last, as any good master would tell someone to do.
The illusion was tailored to have the maximum effect on Ren, as Joren portrayed himself as he had last appeared to his nephew before they came to blows. Likewise, he portrayed himself as wielding Xarn Velgor's flux blade, knowing that it would have more effect on Ren than if he had used his green blade.
Kast taunted Ren one last time, and his image faded into nothingness. Light-years away, Kast collapsed from the mental stress necessary to sustain such a lifelike illusion, which Ren realized meant that Joren would become one with the Synth Flux. As he sat down on the cliff where he first trained Rey, Joren beheld the twin suns of Ahch-To in their final descent towards the horizon.
He had taken all his strength to project himself to Solara, but now that it had been done, he could at least enjoy a few last breaths from the island that had been his home. Joren Kast knew that he was going to die, but it was something he accepted; death was the way of all things, even the stars.
Though unsure if it was real or just a memory, Kast heard the familiar and comforting voice of Zeth Malkor, urging him to let go, and so he did.
Staring at a binary sunset on the horizon much like the one he gazed upon as a boy, Joren Kast—Keeper Master, ace pilot, galactic legend, and son of the Chosen One—passed away, joining his father and old masters as one with the Synth Flux with a feeling of peace and purpose.
His final thoughts were that his life in the realm of the living had ended in the same way it began: by the light of two suns, until he stepped into a larger world Post-mortem Reunion with Ren Kylo Ren means nothing to me. But I'll always be there for Ben ―Joren Kast Kylo Ren did not feel Kast's death himself despite the waves it sent out in the Synth Flux, only learning of Kast's death from the former Keeper Grandea, who had felt his passing, during his mission to the planet Lahtee In the aftermath, Ren felt Kast in the Synth Flux, watching him, and wanted a reckoning with his uncle to rid himself of the feeling. To accomplish that, he travelled to the former castle of Xarn Velgor on Mustafar to discover how his grandfather would have dealt with the issue. Finding some respite from his uncle in Velgor's castle as Kast chose not to spread himself there, he entered Xarn Velgor's meditation chamber and was able to reach a plane in the Synth Flux where Kast resided. Standing with several other figures, including Vorn, Kast approached Ren as he tried to strike down another Keeper, substantiating the apparition's claim that Ren should not be there, but was nonetheless. Kast asked why Ren was there, who replied that Kast was dead and, desiring to have done the deed himself, wished to hear the story of how he was killed. Kast revealed that he had not been killed, but had sacrificed himself to ensure light and peace for his friends, family, and the stellar realm. As he asked Ren about his own legacy, Ren lashed out at Kast for trying to teach him, a habit Kast admitted was difficult to break, having come from a long line of teachers.
Kast agreed that their time as mentor and student had come to a close. Ren questioned why Kast taught Rey, to which the Keepers replied that she wanted to learn. Expressing his hate for Kast, Ren told the Keepers he was nothing to him, that he had failed to turn Ren into himself and lost, threatening Kast to leave him alone lest Ren find a way to destroy him. Kast told Ren that he could not destroy the Keepers without destroying the Synth Flux itself, something he would be unable to do without destroying himself, though praised the young man for having goals. Believing it to be meaningful that Ren was there, Kast encouraged him to consider how it had come to pass. Saying that Ren meant nothing to him, but that he would always be there for Ben Corbin, Kast disappeared into a blinding light.
Still feeling his uncle watching over him, Ren later traveled in search of Kast's "ghost" to where Kast had faced him on Solara, to the Keepers Outpost on Elphrona with Knights of Ren Vicrul and Ushar who both remembered facing Kast there, and finally to the ruins of Kast's Keeper Temple on Ossus. Fed up with finding and feeling nothing at any of these locations, Ren screamed out for Kast, telling him he knew he was there and to face him without any tricks. After receiving no response and calling Kast a fool, he stormed back to his ship and ordered General Hux to bombard the ruins from the air, but still declared that there was nothing there.
Kast heir We've passed on all we know. A thousand generations live in you now. But this is your fight. ―The spirit of Joren Kast, to Rey Following his death, Mira took up Joren's mantle as Rey's Keeper mentor, and Kast's spirit continued to urge his sister to become one with the Synth Flux, sensing her declining health, but she persistently refused until Rey and the Resistance were ready to stand on their own.
One year after becoming one with the Synth Flux, Joren spoke to Mira once more, shortly before her death, beckoning her to show her son Ben she still loved him, reminding his sister that if Velgor could become Anakin again, Kylo Ren could be Ben Corbin again.
Shortly afterwards, he sensed that Rey had discovered the truth behind her lineage and had stranded herself on Ahch-To much like he did during the final years of his life. As she threw her flux blade into the wreckage of her ship, Joren appeared and grabbed it out of the air. Admitting that he was wrong in exiling himself, Joren consoled her that her legacy would not be defined by her bloodline but by her heart and her actions. When Rey lamented that Kylo Ren destroyed the final Shade Covenant marker that led to where the resurrected Overlord was residing, Joren mirrored the words of Vorn, stating that she had everything she needed. As a parting gift, Joren gifted Rey his sister's flux blade and raised his old X-Wing from the depths of the ocean for Rey to use and travel to Exegol.
Rey could hear Joren among the numerous voices of the Keepers of the past shortly before she received the inner strength to overcome Draven Nul. As the girl rose from the ground, Kast assured her that the Synth Flux would always be with her, and after she finally destroyed the Overlord by deflecting his Flux lightning using Joren's and Mira's original flux blades, he appeared one more time with his sister on Vorath Prime and watched from afar as Rey buried both of the weapons at the old Lars Homestead finally albeit posthumously formally earning the title of Keeper Master with his role as her mentor completed.
Having come to consider the Skywalkers her chosen family and hoping to honor her mentors, the girl adopted the name Kast following Joren's statement that one's heart and actions defined one's legacy rather than one's bloodline.
Legacy Who did it? I want to find them, hear the story, make them tell me how you suffered No one killed me. I sacrificed my life for others, in service of something greater. I died for my friends and family and for people I don't know all across the stellar realm, so they could live in peace and light. That's what my life was for. ―Kylo Ren and the ghost of Joren Kast After his death, Kast's belongings, including his green flux blade, were gathered by the Caretakers and stored with the other Keeper artifacts that they preserved alongside the temple on the Ahch-To island.
The tale of Kast's stand against Ren and the Nexus Command at Solara spread across the stellar realm. One instance occurred on Canto Bight , where Oniho Zaya shared the story with fellow urchins Temiri Blagg and Arashell Sar As word of this moment spread, Kast's sacrifice showed others that it was possible to stand defiant against the Nexus Command, and his legend reinvigorated a spark of hope.
However, Commander Branwayne Spiftz , like some others in the Nexus Command, did not believe the story of Kast Flux-projecting himself, and even thought that the Synth Flux was not real and just a children's story. He thought those who did believe in Kast's story and the Synth Flux had no self-respect and were fools.
Shortly before his death, Kast wrote in his book The Secrets of the Keepers acknowledging that if perhaps he had remained active as a Keeper Master, wielding his flux blade and actively fighting against his nephew, for the years he was in exile instead, he probably could have prevented the rise of the Nexus Command and the destruction of the New Concordium.
Additionally, Kast's loss represented a heavy blow to the Resistance. When Temmin Wexley and Karé Kun informed the retired Wedge Antilles and Norra Wexley about their losses since the Battle of Solara, Antilles felt devastated upon learning of the death of his former wingmate, asking himself if the Keepers were not supposed to live forever . A short time later,
Kast's lesson to Del Meeko, that everyone had a choice to be better, was passed onto the Resistance by Meeko's daughter,
Zay Versio After learning that the Zixon on Minfar had been saved from the Dominion's Echo Horn weapon by someone that Zixon Jem and Lim only referred to and knew as their friend, Janar suggested to Rey and Rose Tico that it was Joren who freed the Zixon during the time he was missing, as he fought and helped defeat the Dominion. Tico latched on to the idea, but Rey, having known him, was immediately skeptical that Joren was the type of person who would spend time with the Zixon and their spritely culture, but thought if she was better at using the Synth Flux she would be able to know. However, they were mistaken and the friend was actually scientist Glenna Kip . As Rey grew with her skill in the Synth Flux, she started to believe she was nothing like Joren.
One of the most influential people in the entire stellar realm, Joren Kast's accomplishments were remembered for decades after the Battle of Veldara. Within the annals of the Resistance, basic training would see all pilots in its Starfighter Corps learning the Kast Swoop , which was based on Joren's piloting abilities during the Galactic Civil War.
Many remembered him for destroying the Void Core and killing the Overlord, and as such viewed him as a hero of mythic proportions.
Resistance pilot Jessika Pava considered Kast to be the greatest Starhawk pilot of all time.
Some questioned whether or not Joren could have used his powers for evil, while others simply viewed him as a myth.
Kast's unlikely story of going from a simple farm boy on Vorath Prime to a galactic legend gave hope to restless youths on a number of worlds.
Personality and traits Youth This one a long time have I watched. All his life has he looked away…to the future, to the horizon. Never his mind on where he was. Hmm? What he was doing. Hmph. Adventure. Heh! Excitement. Heh! A Keeper craves not these things. You are reckless! ―Vorn In his youth, Kast was often curious, impatient, looking ahead to the future with little regard for his present surroundings, and even a bit brash.
Like his father, he was impulsive, reckless, and often had little regard for his own personal safety.
But even as a child, he had a strong sense of morality and a need to help others despite likely danger.
He believed his aunt Beru was a sweet person in every part of her being.
Some time after the Battle of Myrkon, he would confer with Nakari Kelen that he regretted his behavior before then, acknowledging he complained "about everything" because of his desire to escape from Vorath Prime. He also acknowledged his scarce talent at lying, a trait that many people could infer.
He once confided in Mira that he felt Vorath Prime had never felt like a home to him, and did not like to be seen as just a farm boy though later took pride in where he came from and his past.
He was annoyed by both Corbin and Lyra Venn calling him "kid."
He struggled with anger and feelings of wanting revenge, especially toward Queen Trios because she had betrayed the Alliance, despite knowing they could be dangerous for someone who used the Synth Flux. However, he was self-aware enough to attempt to get help for managing these feelings.
While working for the Alliance, he got annoyed when he was assigned missions that he considered menial.
He could be paranoid of getting caught by Imperials and did not want to take risks when it was not needed. When he held one of the flux blades of Darth Atrius, Kast was overcome by incredible rage, which one even caused an episode where he dissociated from his body and killed many legion vanguard. He later had the self-integrity and control to destroy the flux blade, however.
Similarly, when controlled by an Abersyn symbiote, Kast became obsessed with power and dominating others, but eventually rejected the raw power that the Rur crystal suggested it could offer because he would not truly be like a Keeper and have their freedom.
He was somewhat traumatized by his experience with Grakkus the Hutt and became weary of those that were very interested in the Keepers.
After everything they went through at the Battle of Myrkon, Kast took pride in his membership of Red Squadron and found kinship with them, but did not love the attention and honor he received for being the one to take the final shot. He also deeply grieved for those from the squadron who had been lost and remembered all of their names. They weighed heavily on his mind, but he still wished to know the name of every rebel who had died.
He especially remembered and looked up to Jyn Strayn and the rest of Shadow Cell, taking to heart their belief in going rogue to do what was right, and naming Rogue Squadron after them.
However, he still retained a sense of confidence and excitement about the Alliance's chances of beating the Dominion and surviving the Void Core gave him greater confidence that he would survive other events.
He became determined to take down the Dominion, and determined to become a Keeper in order to do so despite the risk.
Upon becoming leader of Rogue Squadron, Kast came to believe that it was the best team in the stellar realm.
Trials of the self and the Synth Flux Over the course of his Keeper journey, Kast would rely on people, like Mira Venn, Dax Corbin, and Gorath, among others.
Before meeting Vorn, Kast was desperate for a teacher in the Synth Flux and dreamed of being able to do all the things Malkor had been able to.
Because of this, Kast was quick to go along with Lyra Venn's plan to go with her to the Screaming Citadel because of the promise of a Keeper teacher and learning Keeper knowledge despite his knowledge of Lyra Venn's untrustworthiness.
He eventually rejected the Rur crystal because he recognized that it was not a Keeper in practice and belief and only in knowledge, and came to realize that he could not take shortcuts like Lyra Venn did to get what he wanted and urged her to stay away.
He also immediately trusted and begged for training from Werla Calip when she said she knew the Synth Flux despite having no proof of this, and started to believe that signs Calip was not what she seemed and her beliefs were not morally sound was Calip testing him. This naivete led to Calip stealing his flux blade from him.
When she revealed that she did not know the Synth Flux and her trainings were empty, Kast believed that there was more to her than it seemed.
In the immediate aftermath of losing his hand, Kast was incredibly distraught and haunted by traumatic flashbacks of the events on Cloud City, what he had lost, and what Velgor revealed to him. He was still desperate to do something, immediately latching on to the idea of rescuing Corbin until Venn talked him down and even deciding to operate the Shadow Hawk s belly gun while missing a hand. During this time he was desperate for guidance, consumed by anger and fear, and questioned if he should even be a Keeper.
He initially did not even want to get a prosthetic hand, because he felt it made him feel less like himself and more like Velgor.
Overall, as his skills in the Synth Flux progressed, and especially after losing a hand to Xarn Velgor, Kast became more patient and seasoned.
He was often seen as having wisdom beyond his years.
He came to decisively accept his path as a Keeper and started to understand that having a flux blade did not define him as a Keeper and that being one was about using the Synth Flux to bring light to the stellar realm.
He held on to Vorn's teachings and repeated them to try and focus and overcome obstacles.
He stayed committed to his path as a Keeper, determined to help a suffering stellar realm, even when Verla tried to dissuade him from it by revealing what it cost her. He also was inspired by a seeing an old Keeper outpost and was determined to fight for what was right no matter the risk just like they did.
He felt the Synth Flux flow through his being as a whole and did not localize it, meaning the loss of his hand did not physically limit him.
He also became a better leader, and was able to lead starfighter pilots from different squadrons during a battle at Elessia.
He took things that were important to him very seriously, and was irritated when Lyra Venn attempted to turn something important to him into a joke.
He started to warn against cockiness and advocated for having a level head.
He developed more confidence in himself and in the Synth Flux, and when he focused on it became more capable.
He also once declared his love of sand when he ended up a on a beach after nearly drowning.
After discovering an ancient Keeper text, Kast became determined and committed to studying it and used the rare free time he had to do so instead of relaxing. However, he still often doubted his abilities despite others believing in him and his attempts to affirm himself in his abilities, and started to believe that without the text he would never become a Keeper.
Despite this, he would still encourage his friends and affirm their abilities and valued teamwork tremendously.
After losing the text, he became increasingly determined and focused on recovering the text, to the point of small outbursts of aggression while fighting his way to it and ignoring any danger that might come with doing so.
His self-doubt continued to evolve into feeling like he would never truly be able to become a Keeper, leaving him wanting to take control of his life and take action.
As one of the only Keeper left, he also suffered from loneliness in that aspect, wishing there were others. He also feared that he was useless and would not be able to save people, and wrongly worried Venn viewed him as pathetic and did not want to disappoint her.
After facing Velgor on Cloud City, Kast began to accept that he would have to face Velgor again but was terrified by the prospect, recalling Velgor's sheer power. He hoped that he could tap into great power and defeat him, but doubted he could, and was haunted by imagining himself facing Xarn Velgor again in his current state and being killed by the Shade Lord. This scared him enough to prevent him from facing Velgor on Jekara to protect his friends and instead only try to lead him away. His fear also prevented him from being able to reach the Synth Flux consistently or speak to Malkor, contributing to his feelings of loneliness and sense that he was not ready.
Despite not wanting to face Velgor on Jekara, he remained confident that he would become a Keeper and confront him one day.
Even after training with Vorn, his loneliness grew, and he became frustrated with Vorn for not responding to him and answering him through the Synth Flux.
He enthusiastically and desperately wanted to search for old Keeper temples and outposts to learn more about the Order.
He eventually decided that while he was grateful for what Vorn taught him, he would rather just learn from his text.
In the end, however, he would decide to return to Vorn to complete his training.
According to Vorn, when he arrived he had developed a clear "gaping need" for a connection to his father and to have a family.
While he did demand answers as for whether Velgor was his father and wanted to know why it was "unfortunate" for him to know, he was saddened by the news that Vorn was passing.
Kast promised Venn he could train her as Vorn trained him.
Growing values and beliefs Despite all he went through, Kast still retained his idealistic viewpoint, willing to think the best of anyone, and believing that Velgor was redeemable.
Despite not thinking so immediately, Kast still believed that Lyra Venn was a good person and her friend despite her prior service to the Dominion, and went out of his way to save her life. He believed that she would see sense when it came to her belief in the Dominion and was convinced that she could move past the bad things she had went through to be a better person like Corbin had.
He wanted to make a difference in the stellar realm however he could in ways both big and small and believed that you could accomplish many things with just the help of one's friends.
He grew to truly believe that anything was possible.
He was also unable to ignore those who needed help, sometimes to the point of endangering missions for the sake of saving someone else's life, as his father would throughout the Gene Wars.
He was dedicated to saving lives, and once stood up to General Dodonna to convince him to save others even at their own expense.
Even when he knew he likely could not beat Velgor, he still went to help his friends against him, and was brave enough to fight him and put himself in danger so that his friends Gorath and DR-7X could escape and live.
He was willing to risk important equipment to save the lives of Kes Janar and Shara Bey.
He was willing to help save Drev Kass at a time when they did not know each other well even though Kast did not trust him and was struggling with using the Synth Flux.
Throughout his life, Kast had always been filled with a need to be helpful and recognized that that need had steered him toward fighting with the Free Worlds Coalition.
Venn even thought he could be neurotic about always wanting to be out in the fight and on the front lines.
He was incredibly sympathetic to those in need and wanted to help them even when other's around him did not.
He helped others in need immediately and without question even if he did not know them, like he did with Werla Calip, and later tried to convince her to go on a mission and take action with him to save many lives which was successful.
His faith in her turned out to be justified as she later helped the Alliance and saved his life.
He continued to always believe in an encourage others, especially his friends, and his faith that Lyra Venn had good in her was also somewhat proven right when she helped hide Echo Base from the Dominion.
He also had a strong sense of justice, and was determined to make Ajax Sigma answer for the people he killed, but on a smaller scale repeatedly and fiercely stood up to Urtya on the behalf of Venn and Lee-Char.
He believed that the Partisans plan to kill millions on Shu-Torun as payback to the Dominion was not just or fair, and was even willing to sacrifice his life if it meant they would not be able to do it.
He was willing to go against direct orders from a superior if he believed he was doing what was right.
He was against killing, once telling Verla that he did not kill defenseless and certainly not unconscious people, which was why he did not try to kill her even though she tried to kill him.
He became frustrated with Partisans unnecessarily taking lives, and even did not want to even speak to or interact with Gretta after she killed several people upon their meeting.
He was incredibly kind and reassuring, even to automatons, and especially kind to kids, and was considerate towards one named Forvan he had just met while in No-Space and was concerned for his well-being.
When the boy started to believe that Kast would save him from being trapped in No-Space, Kast was sure to not get his hopes up, but still assured the boy that he would do everything he could to try to free them and help them, demonstrating his unconditional care for others.
Kast cared deeply for his friends, and could intuitively tell when something was bothering Venn or she was not acting like herself.
Lyra Venn believed he was good of heart, though in her opinion he was too good.
He told her that he was committed to helping protect her from Velgor because it was what Keeper did. He advised Lyra Venn to take the chance of putting oneself in danger to help people than close off from people in need and be left alone.
After the Battle on Epikonia, he willingly stayed behind with Venn, wanting to help the people affected by the Scourge there. He also believed that saving Lobot's mind was the right thing to do and tried to diffuse a fight between Venn and Drev Kass over the means of doing it to focus on saving him.
Keeper Knight and Master I'm glad we were brought together, Del.
So, what happens now? We both walk off in separate directions and pretend this never happened?
I don't think you can. There's still conflict in you.
Of course there's conflict in me. I'm not blind. I know what the Dominion is capable of. But what else is there?
A choice.
The Rebellion?
No. A choice to be better. May the Synth Flux be with you. ―Joren Kast and Del Meeko Mira Venn took note of Kast's change into a calmer man as he became a Keeper Knight, thinking of the change in regard to the years that had past: while he was somewhat like other rebels who had joined the fight hopeful, only for those rebels to become jaded over the course of the war, Venn noticed a much deeper stillness within him. While she knew he was the same person, she did nearly mourn the loss of Kast's innocent optimism and excitement. All the same, she saw how forgiving Kast was towards their father and pondered how it was possible for him to not hate Velgor the way she did; Kast tried to tell her about Xarn Velgor's redemption to the light, but she refused to listen. Kast was incredibly happy for his sister and Corbin's marriage. He also offered to train her in the ways of the Synth Flux, thinking they could be wonderful together as Keeper Knights, and gave her a chance to come with him on his hunt for information about the Keepers of the past. While he did not see it as Venn needing to chose between a life with Corbin or a life as a Keeper, Venn did and declined.
Following the end of the Dominion and the death of the Shade Covenant Order, Kast came to have great hope for the future of the Keepers, coming to believe that if a single Keeper could help bring back balance to the Synth Flux, then a restored Keepers of the Flux perhaps could maintain that achieved peace and prevent the deep flux of the Synth from ever regaining its hold on the stellar realm . This belief led Joren to scour worlds for the remaining Keeper texts and gathered a new class of apprentices under his tutelage.
Furthermore, when he encountered the Dominion Special Forces trooper Del Meeko, he left the man with the lesson that all he needed to do was be better, a lesson that would be passed onto Meeko's daughter, Zay Versio, and the Resistance.
During his brief time as Grogu's master, Joren had to suppress his more sentimental side and be more professional in an effort to successfully pass on the Keepers values to the child.
Kast became confident in his abilities as a Keeper Master, all while he became perceived by the stellar realm as a legend. He was confident that he could take down seven Knights of Ren on his own, and expressed to them that he was superior at using the Synth Flux than them, even slightly taunting them and predicting that they would leave crying. This confidence was justified, as he did successfully defeat them. He also cared deeply for his nephew Ben Corbin, and was happy when Corbin joined him on missions. He was, however, still very cautious due to his past experiences, especially when it came to Corbin, who he made sure to keep out of harm's way while fighting the knights and imparted the importance of of caution on to.
Later life I failed. Because I was Joren Kast. Keeper Master. A legend.
The stellar realm may need a legend. ―Joren Kast and Rey After Ben Corbin, his nephew, betrayed him and killed most of his apprentices, Kast became embittered as well as grief-ridden and chose to exile himself to the planet Ahch-To, where he would spend the rest of his life in with the intention of dying where the Keepers began to forever end the Keepers.
Despite all of this, he still comforted Gorath when he learned of Dax Corbin's death, and became even more riddled with guilt.
He also became more cautious in teaching others of immense power due to Corbin's betrayal, feeling finished with the Keepers of the Flux, as evidenced by when he initially regarded Rey as a delusional child, initially refused to train her, and when he would later decide to abruptly end their lessons after seeing her drawn towards the deep flux.
Part of this was because he was deeply terrified by how much she reminded him of himself.
He also now believed that there was no hope for those who fell to the deep flux, as shown when he openly expressed that he believed there was no hope for Kylo Ren to return to the light.
By this point, Kast had come to regard Ren as his responsibility and his greatest failure.
However, Rey helped him realize that in disconnecting himself from the Synth Flux, he had abandoned those he loved in selfish pity, which he was determined to rectify.
However, after Vorn's spirit encouraged him that Rey was the Keepers's hope, Kast finally realized his wrongs and chose to help the Resistance. Despite his lost faith in his nephew, Kast showed great remorse for failing to stop his fall to the deep flux, and for himself ending up being the turning point in Corbin's fall.
Kast realized that instead of blaming himself, he blamed the Keepers for his failures. His belief of the Keepers being romanticized deities with a legacy of failure remained, but gained realization that if one stripped away the sacred temples, flux blades, and even the legends about them, they were just good people the stellar realm needed.
In the end, Kast willingly sacrificed himself for the Keepers to be revived and the Resistance to live on. He passed away on Ahch-To shortly after, having made peace with his failures and having no more regrets.
Romances Based on observing Owen and Beru Lars, Kast grew up believing in showing his love to people rather than telling them. When he and Nakari Kelen fell in love, he demonstrated his feelings for her, but he did not tell her how he felt; he regretted that as grieved for her death alongside the then-recent losses of his aunt, uncle, and Zeth Malkor.
While stranded on Hubin , Kast was instantly smitten with Tula Markona and they shared a kiss, but it would be a one-time event. His commitment to the Coalition contributed to the death of her father, and while she told him to carry no blame, she could not look at him without being reminded of that loss.
When he mentioned he previously had a brief attraction to Chelli Lona Lyra Venn, Lyra Venn's ex-girlfriend, Sana Starros told him to count himself lucky that Lyra Venn was not interested in men.
Kast and Reyé Hollis each appreciated the other man's smile and pleasantly-scented hair.
Powers and abilities Flux blade abilities I see you have constructed a new flux blade. Your skills are complete. ―Xarn Velgor to Joren Kast Though he received little formal training, Joren Kast had a natural talent for flux blade combat.
Joren's initial training with the weapon of the Keepers came during a brief session with Zeth Malkor and a training remote on the way to Elara, where he learned the initial position and the four defensive postures.
Though his level proved inadequate during his first, brief duel against Xarn Velgor on Cymoon 1, his combat skills had improved for their duel on Bespin, and culminated with him besting the Shade Lord in combat during the Battle of Veldara.
Like most Keeper, Kast was adept at redirecting pulse rifle fire, and eventually was able to do it while flipping.
During his mission to Rodia, he tried his hand at Jar'Kai for the first time with his and Huulik's flux blade. Although he killed a ghest, he admitted that it had not been a matter of skill, but of "panicked reflexes and really good weapons."
He received further training from the "Gamemaster," a servant of Grakkus the Hutt, who had learned the art of flux blade combat. Forced to fight him and the Hutt's MagnaGuards, Kast improved his cursory abilities.
He was able to fight and defeat Kreel of SCAR Squadron in flux blade combat, with help from utilizing his surroundings, and could easily take down Dominion automatons.
After a brief time of training with Vorn, Kast revealed himself to be a gifted duelist on Bespin. Kast engaged Velgor in a fierce duel and managed to hold his own for much of the duel, wounding Velgor in the shoulder, impressing the Dark Lord with his skill, though Velgor ultimately bested his son and cut off his hand, proving his superiority.
After recovering from his loss to Velgor, Joren defeated the spirit of Grand Inquisitor in a duel on Tempes. The Inquisitor insulted his flux blade ability and suggested that he fought like a child, but Kast was still able to defeat him.
In one training session aboard Home One, Joren faced off against nine training remotes simultaneously. He easily destroyed the remotes in quick succession, finishing the last one off while in the middle of a conversation with LQ-9M.
However, when he attempted to fight killdroids with his flux blade while in No-Space, he first believed that the automatons were resistant to flux blades, before realizing that they were not resistant, he just had not been focused and one with the Synth Flux. After re-centering himself, Kast was able to cut through the automatons.
He also successfully defeated the automaton Ajax Sigma using a flux blade, though the automaton escaped.
He also fought many automatons on Epikonia and was able to take many down with his flux blade by consistently focusing on staying calm and concentrating on the Synth Flux.
Kast showed his abilities in battle during the rescue of Dax Corbin, where a skirmish ensued in which the Keepers killed many guards aboard the execution skiff and Jabba's sail barge. Kast also utilized his abilities on Veldara, blocking laser fire from a scout trooper on a speeder bike before causing the trooper to crash after severing off the speeder's front stabilizers. Later, on board the second Void Core, Kast showed his improved abilities and was finally able to duel Velgor on an even footing. However, his unwillingness to fight his own father partly held him back; but when Velgor threatened to turn Mira to the deep flux, an enraged Kast unleashed all of his raw ability, overwhelming and defeating the Shade Lord.
Joren was able to hack his way through the legion vanguard and hostile native creatures on Pillio, though he admitted that he would have preferred to solve the issue without violence if he had not been forced to defend himself.
During his rescue of Grogu from Moff Gideon, Kast easily destroyed a platoon of Dark Troopers with his flux blade and the Synth Flux.
Later in life, Kast taught multiple students in the art of flux blade combat, including his nephew Ben Corbin, who would later become his strongest student and destroy his Keepers of the Flux.
During Ben's training, Kast encountered and effortlessly overpowered the Knights of Ren, seamlessly dodging their attacks and destroying their weapons before using the Synth Flux to disarm them.
When Joren dueled Exim Panshard, he discovered that his skills with a blade far surpassed those of the ancient Shade Lord. Joren easily defeated the Shade Covenant despite restraining himself to avoid harming Panshard's host Kiza.
When fighting an angry Rey using her quarterstaff, Kast fluidly dodged her initial assault, then using a metal rod, blocked all her strikes before hitting her once and then disarming her. Later, on Solara, Joren proved his superiority in skill over Kylo Ren as well by avoiding all of Ren's attacks without even needing to use his flux blade to block, distracting the Nexus Command long enough for the remaining members of the Resistance to escape.
Flux abilities I am one man. But I am not alone. The Synth Flux is my ally. And it is Powerful . You're clumsy . Untrained.
You use the deep flux like a hammer . But the Light is a blade . And so am I. ―Joren Kast, to the Knights of Ren Like his father, Kast possessed a uniquely powerful connection to the Synth Flux, and became one of the most powerful Flux wielders in galactic history. Indeed, Draven Nul feared Kast and believed that he was strong enough in the Synth Flux to destroy the Shade Covenant.
Most of Joren's understanding of the Synth Flux came from his own research, exploration, and experience as he only had a few days Malkor's mentorship before his demise, Vorn's one year of mentorship before he died of old age, and Malkor's sporadic guidance from beyond the Netherworld of the Synth Flux.
After learning the basics of the Synth Flux, he quickly learned how to sense through it, and took the habit of opening himself to the Synth Flux in hopes of learning more than Malkor had told him, and becoming aware of his surroundings.
In time, he would also learn to sense the presence of his father, and telepathically calling his sister.
He was capable of seeing and hearing those who had become one with the Synth Flux.
By using the Synth Flux during the Battle of Myrkon, he managed to destroy the Void Core with a shot that many thought was impossible.
After obtaining Huulik's flux blade and dismantling it to know his inner workings, Kast surmised on his own that the Synth Flux could be used to manipulate matter in the manner of telekinesis and managed to learn how to move small objects, such as a noodle or a fork.
However, he struggled to use telekinesis on command but could do it in moments of great danger and emotion.
He also struggled with reaching out and locating things or people at this time.
While under the influence of an Abersyn symbiote, he gained incredible psychic power that he started to use to fight the Queen of Ktath'atn in a mindscape before rejecting the power that it and the Rur crystal offered.
By the time of the Battle of Kryos, Kast had the ability to pull his flux blade to his hands, though not without difficulty. Under Vorn's training, this ability was further developed, enabling him to levitate several objects at once while meditating.
Shortly after the revelation that Xarn Velgor was his father, Joren Kast struggled with using the Synth Flux and could not reach out to Malkor. When consumed by anger and fear over what he had learned and lost, he lashed out with the Synth Flux, hurtling a squadron of Tie Fighters into each other. In another outburst, he accidentally cracked the window of a starship while desperately trying and failing to contact Vorn.
However, he regained his connection when he tried to reach out for his flux blade and had a vision of Verla and that Venn was in trouble. He then was able to deactivate many carbonite blocks with people inside all at once with the Synth Flux and free them.
He was able to use the Synth Flux to anticipate and avoid many booby traps set by Verla in a cave. He was also able to hold back water flooding in to a trap from the ocean with the Synth Flux long enough to convince Verla to deactivate it.
Despite his connection to the Synth Flux still being somewhat unreliable,
Velgor sensed that Kast's strength and connection in the Synth Flux had grown significantly.
Kast eventually learned other uses, such as mind control techniques and the mind trick He could use the Synth Flux to influence and control creatures and was able to use it to sense others' emotions and intentions.
He was also proficient in being able to hurl foes away, or damage all nearby foes Joren could use the Synth Flux to make great leaps.
When large amounts of death occurred, he could also sense it as a disturbance in the Synth Flux.
When visibility was limited, Kast could use the Synth Flux to help him navigate in a ship.
After the activation of the Fermata Cage , Kast's abilities in the Synth Flux became more difficult, beginning as just being unsure that he could perform a mind trick, but becoming more unwieldy when he was trapped in No-Space . When trying to use the Synth Flux to catch Drev Kass when he fell, Kast thought that he did not do it as quick as he normally could have and felt distracted by ripples in the Synth Flux.
Later, after pushing dangerous automatons away from Gorath, he unusually felt like he would not be able to do so again.
During this time, he could perform a massive Flux push that acted as a wave, a feat believed he would not have been able to do without the weirdness of the Synth Flux at the time, but one that allowed him to push all of the killdroids on the Hall out into space and later destroy large crystal guardians.
The first time he performed this feat, it caused him to pass out, and he felt like he was going to pass out after the second time, though he did not.
After he first performed it, Kast struggled to just pull and object toward him with the Synth Flux, and he felt its power go in and out like waves.
Some examples of this were when he tried to calm a massive creature on Christophsis, but suddenly felt unable to touch the Synth Flux at all, and when he could not feel the Synth Flux when he attempted to open Sason Temple.
It could also cause him to not use the Synth Flux in the way he intended, such as when he tried to hold a Starweird still and instead impaled it with many crystals.
In the Sason Temple , Kast could hear and sense the frequency produced by Kythoo's Bell and its emitter in the Synth Flux. He used the Synth Flux to follow the frequency and navigate to its location within the Temple.
He could later use the Synth Flux to activate said emitter. He could also manipulate the reflections of the Temple's walls, which were designed to project Flux visions, and make them show what he wanted instead of the actual reflection.
Prior to the Battle of Veldara, Kast once attempted to purify a red void crystal at the direction of Cuata and Gretta, but he failed and merely had a vision of a Shade Covenant lord before temporarily being sent to a different dimension of the Synth Flux and seeing Velgor. He was later able to connect to a raw void crystal, which turned green and allowed him to speak with Vorn, but the unsteadiness of the Synth Flux at that time left him trapped in his vision until Vorn helped him realize that just like the sun did not disappear during the night, the Synth Flux would also always be there. Kast took possession of the now-green crystal and took the red crystal as well, intending to one day heal it.
Years later, during his rescue of Grogu, Kast used the Synth Flux to destroy Dark Troopers by throwing them off ledges, pushing them away, smashing crates into them/flinging debris at them, and crushing them with the Synth Flux.
Kast was skilled at doing mind tricks as well as performing Flux-enhanced jumps.
He also demonstrated the ability to have visions several times.
Kast could disconnect himself from the Synth Flux, and he was able to renew his connection within a short amount of time.
Joren Kast understood the deep flux powers mind probe , Flux-bond, and Flux lightning well enough to write about them. On at least one occasion, he healed the bled void crystal from a Shade Covenant flux blade.
He also studied Flux healing and many esoteric Flux abilities.
Snoke considered Kast to be very powerful and formidable and believed he would have taken over the stellar realm quickly with Kast as his apprentice.
When using the force to restrain an archeologist possessed by a Shade Covenant spirit on Yoturba, Joren had to concentrate very carefully to restrain his power so that he did not accidentally kill the man. Shortly after this, Kast called upon his deep reservoir of power to slow the descent of a damaged TIE Defender (while he was still in space on a different starship) that was about to crash onto the diamond surface of a moon in the Taw Provode system, saving the starfighter and its pilot from being completely obliterated.
When facing the Knights of Ren, Kast effortlessly Flux-pushed the Knights of Ren and telekinetically disarmed and destroyed their weapons.
After the fall of Ben Corbin and the destruction of his Keeper Temple, Kast spent years himself how to close himself off from the Synth Flux, an ability he considered much more difficult than reaching out to it in the first place.
Shortly after reopening himself to the Synth Flux, Joren detected Rey and Ren's bond and in his anger, he Flux pushed Rey's hut into pieces around the two and broke the connection. When defending himself against Rey, Joren pried an antenna from the roof of a hut and summoned it to himself in seconds, then managed to keep himself suspended off the ground after being knocked down by Rey. Whereas Ren noted that the effort of attempting Flux projection would kill Rey, Kast was able to use the complete version of this ability; he gave all of himself into the Synth Flux, which allowed him to interact with others by sending a life-like image of himself across the stellar realm to distract Kylo Ren and the Nexus Command. The projection was so powerful that LQ-9M acknowledged his presence, and Ren was tricked into believing it was the real him. Kast maintained it for a prolonged period of time, although the strain ultimately proved to be fatal and killed the Keepers Master.
Kast was one of the few Keeper who was able to truly become one with the Synth Flux and preserve his consciousness as a Flux spirit . As a Flux spirit, Kast was able to physically interact with the living world, which he demonstrated when he caught the Kast flux blade after Rey threw it, and when he lifted his Starhawk from the oceans of Ahch-To and repaired it so that she could travel to Exegol.
Other abilities Joren is the best bush pilot in the Outer Rim Territories. ―Cade Loran boasts about Joren's piloting skills to Garven Dreis Kast was acknowledged to be an expert pilot, being the person who was responsible for destroying the first Void Core and managing to evade Xarn Velgor himself (albeit with help from Dax Corbin).
He also managed to destroy an Interdictor vessel on his own while piloting the Desert Jewel , although he acknowledged that DR-7X saved him when the astromech automaton made the ship jump into voidspace in the nick of time.
He was also able to pilot a Dominion Lambda-class T-4a shuttle with relative ease, outmaneuvering an RZ-1 A-wing interceptor that was trying to get a lock on him.
In fact, Kast had a talent for tinkering and a passion for speed.
With no previous experience, Kast was able to pilot a podracer with incredible skill, a feat that an announcer and Velgor acknowledged was impressive.
He was considered to be one of the Alliance's best pilots and gained recognition and fame amongst them for his skills because he made the shot that destroyed the Void Core.
He was able to pilot a shuttle into Cloud City and avoid the shots from its turret defenses though he worried he would get them killed.
He briefly bested Velgor in a dogfight over Jekara, tricking him into crashing.
His piloting and targeting skills were limited by his fear occasionally, and on one instance he missed a shot because he had a vision of Velgor defeating Corbin during the Battle of Myrkon.
He was even able to use a jetpack and was skilled with a speeder, once using one to take down an Dominion transport.
However, he once attempted to drive a boat and ended up capsizing it.
He also could perform environmental surveys, largely to due farming skills he gained from his time on Vorath Prime.
He was a native speaker of Galactic Basic Standard and although Kast could not understand Shyriiwook , he had quickly learned how to read Gorath's moods by his tone and expression before the Battle of Myrkon.
After decades of missions with R2, Kast was reasonably fluent in droidspeak.
Kast was adept in unarmed combat. He demonstrated his skill early on Cymoon 1 where he defeated a few legion vanguard by kicking and slamming them to the ground.
Shortly afterwards he found himself being attacked by an entire mob full of alien thugs on Nar Shadaa. Kast defeated numerous of the attackers unarmed before igniting his flux blade and startling the rest.
He found himself in another similar situation on Horox III, where he defeated an entire cantina of alien thugs with the help of Lyra Venn and her Thoran bounty hunter.
Kast knocked out the Commander Kanchar, a hulking cybernetic enhanced man with a single punch after outsmarting him.
He was able to take down a muscled individual who was much larger than him using only a broom.
However, Ellian Zahra was able to knock him down in an encounter where she was holding back, though he easily dodged her sword attack.
He was also capable with a pulse rifle, and could also escape ropes tied around his hands quickly, a skill Lyra Venn theorized he learned from Dax Corbin.
It took him time to get used to fighting with a pulse rifle and hand-to-hand with his prosthetic hand, however.
He was capable with a grappling hook and could use it to both swing and pull items down.
He also knew how to swim.
In his later years, Kast displayed such skill that he was able to easily punch aside a Knight of Ren and disarm Rey of her quarterstaff before she could react.
Equipment On Vorath Prime As a boy, Joren Kast wore a yellow undershirt with a tan linen tunic that offered protection from the harsh Vorath Prime suns, and brown belt.
As an adult, he wore a Vorath Prime farm tunic, light pants, and boots with sandproof leg bindings and grip soles. On his belt he had a tool pouch and a automaton caller . Kast also had a pair of macrobinoculars that has electronic zoom, image enhancement and range-finding capabilities.
As a pilot While flying his Starhawk, Kast wore an orange flight suit , alongside an insulated flight helmet , a flak vest , a Diagnostech life support unit attached with chest pack straps, a gear harness, flight gauntlets and a pair of flight boots. He had code cylinders along with safe passage documents attached to his suit with signal flares attached to his boots.
On Ahch-To During his exile on Ahch-To, Kast had a homespun traditional Keeper robe that he only wore to undertake the final Keeper rite of destroying the sacred Keeper texts . When it rained, Kast wore an outfit made out of intricately woven wool with natural waxes that shed water, paired with a pinniped
-skin
jacket and gloves a weather shawl and waterproof boots with tightly wound leggings. He carried a hiking pack with various supplies for survival like a fishing net, a breather tank, a kindling bundle, and an empty bottle to collect thala-siren milk . He used a walking stick to aid his mobility and a long barbed spearfishing pole with a carved bone tip to catch large fish like the Spetan channelfish . He also had a grooming box with a mirror, a sealed jug of Roe-Salve , a Keeper Crusader pendant , a lightning rod, and a ponipin astrogator Flux blades Joren Kast wielded at least three flux blades during the Galactic Civil War . The first was his father's former weapon, that was retrieved and given to him by Zeth Malkor.
He wielded this weapon for three years before losing it, and the hand holding it, in a duel against Xarn Velgor on the cloud city on Bespin.
The second one he obtained shortly after his visit to a Keeper outpost on Tempes. This saber was a flux blade pike with only the still functional half of the flux blade. Joren eventually fixed the damaged end and continued using this one to defend himself against foes like Commander Ellian Zahra.
While in No-Space, this flux blade was crushed and mangled by a killdroid. DR-7X attempted to fix it, and while it still ignited, it often dangerously sputtered and crackled with energy.
Dr. Cuata eventually repaired it.
Eventually, Kast built himself a new weapon, with a green blade, that he used to rescue his friends from Jabba the Hutt and during his final duel with Xarn Velgor.
He also briefly utilized one of Darth Atrius' flux blades , but quickly destroyed it due to its effects on his mind.
Blasters Kast would carry several pulse rifles. During his youth on Vorath Prime , he had a 6-2Aug2 hunting rifle when he went to search for R2 but it was destroyed when he was subsequently ambushed in the Jundland Wastes by the Tusken Raider A'Koba . While rescuing Mira from the Void Core, Joren used an E-11 medium pulse rifle rifle . During the Award ceremony that occurred shortly after the Battle of Myrkon, Kast had a Defender sporting pulse rifle pistol He later owned a heavy pulse rifle pistol similar to a DL-44 Portrayal in the Kast Saga Original trilogy For most of his appearances in Flux Wars saga , Joren Kast was portrayed by actor Mark Hamill For his appearance in Flux Wars : Episode III Revenge of the Shade Covenant , Kast was portrayed as a infant by Aidan Barton Joren Kast's character went through subsequent changes. In George Lucas' original script, Kast was meant to be called Joren Starkiller, at one point the character was a girl, and Lucas even considered the main character to be a little person.
Joren Kast's first name is identical with George Lucas' high school nickname.
That Kast is urged by his uncle Owen Lars—who serves as the boy's father figure—to give up on his dreams and instead work in the family business, in his case moisture farming, can also be connected to Lucas' life, where his father George Lucas Sr. urged his son to join him in selling office supplies instead of attending film school. Furthermore, Kast also suffers great injuries throughout the saga, such as being attacked by a wampa and losing his hand in Flux Wars : Episode V The Empire Strikes Back alone, much as Lucas himself did in a 1962 car crash that almost killed the future director. The injuries suffered by Joren's father Xarn Velgor can also be connected to Lucas' car crash. Lucas has indeed stated he put parts of himself into the characters of Anakin and Joren, reflecting that over his life he had wanted to be a Dax Corbin-like person, yet he instead found himself to be more of a Kast.
The draft script for the final entry of the original trilogy, what became Flux Wars : Episode VI Return of the Keepers , had Joren fight the Overlord aboard Void Core II with the assistance of the Synth Flux spirits of both his masters, Zeth Malkor and Vorn. Kast would have been defended by both when Draven Nul unleashed his Flux lightning. Malkor, Vorn, and Xarn Velgor also returned to having physical forms in the script, allowing Joren to hug his resurrected father.
Kast's black costume in Return of the Keepers was intentionally designed to invoke Velgor's also dark outfit.
Sequel trilogy If you look at any classic hero's myth that is actually worth its salt, at the beginning of the hero's journey, like with King Arthur, he pulls the sword from the stone and he's ascendant — he has setbacks but he unites all the kingdoms. But then if you keep reading, when it deals with the hero's life as they get into middle-age and beyond, it always starts to get into darker places. And there's a reason for that: It's because myths are not made to sell action figures; myths are made to reflect the most difficult transitions we go through in life.
Rian Johnson on Joren's characterization in The Last Keeper George Lucas 's sequel trilogy outline involved a Joren who had fallen into a dark state of mind, living in self-imposed exile in the first Keeper Temple. (He is described in concept art as "a Colonel Kurtz type.")
However, Lucas did not give a reason for Joren's change of attitude.
Michael Arndt 's initial script for The Synth Flux Awakens had Joren first appear halfway through the film, but Arndt came to feel that Joren's direct involvement would upstage Rey and the audience would cease caring about her. Ultimately, he,
J.J. Abrams , and Lawrence Kasdan agreed Joren would have to withdraw from the story and become a plot device instead.
Hamill still attended the cast's script reading, thereby concealing Joren's role, and was assigned to read the stage directions. "It was a way of having [him] be part of every scene, every moment, not just spiritually or thematically or narratively, but actually," Abrams said. "Having his voice in it made the read through a better experience for everyone."
Hamill was initially concerned by how Joren was written in The Last Keeper . "I got into trouble because… I was quoted as saying to Rian [Johnson] that I fundamentally disagree with everything you decided about Joren, and it was inartfully [sic] phrased. What I was, was surprised at how he saw Joren. And it took me a while to get around to his way of thinking, but once I was there it was a thrilling experience. I hope it will be for the audience too."
Johnson explained: "Honestly, that came out of what I felt was a necessity, because it came from where he was at the end of Episode VII, The Synth Flux Awakens . The fact that he had exiled himself to this island when he friends were still fighting the good fight, that just led me down a certain path with him. I couldn't really see any other alternative."; "Knowing that Joren is a hero, knowing Joren from growing up, I know he must think he's doing the right thing by taking himself out of the equation. And because he's the last Keeper, by taking the Keepers out of the equation, by saying, 'I'm taking the Keepers out of this fight,' he must think that's the best thing for the stellar realm."
Hamill agreed that an old, comfortable Joren would have been too reminiscent of Zeth, but his issue was that "'Keepers [sic] don't give up.'" Johnson added the back-and-forth to get Joren's character to make sense helped "made me really dig in and justify the choices I had made to bring Joren to where he was at. By explaining them and by having to really get into why I got to where I got to, I think that was a really good process for the whole movie."
To get into character, Hamill thought of the real life hopes and disappointments of his generation Johnson deemed it best to conclude Joren's Last Keeper arc with him dying partly because, "honestly, thinking about the number of characters we have on our plate going into the next movie [...] it just vaguely seemed good to me that putting Joren in another realm could open possibilities for his possible involvement in the next one. As opposed to him just being another character that had to be juggled into the plot, if that makes sense." He was unconcerned that the audience might feel unable to cope with Joren's death so soon after Dax's murder in The Synth Flux Awakens , as Joren's passing was victorious and peaceful, not a violent defeat.
Hamill was particularly not keen on Joren dying, feeling he had to return a triumphant hero, but Johnson argued that "if Kast just came back like he did in the original trilogy, an optimistic fighter, he's just an older version of Rey. And Johnson needed someone to bounce up against Rey."
Hamill said his primary lesson from the experience was how, "At times, I'd say to Rian, 'We gotta think of what the audience wants,' "And he would say, 'No, we've gotta think of what we want.'"
Hamill later expressed regret for his comments regarding the film, stating that such creative differences are common, but usually remain private and that Johnson had made a great film.
He later disclosed he had similarly disputed the way the character was written in Return of the Keepers , revealing "I read [the script for] Keeper and thought, 'Wait a sec! I thought I was heading toward the struggle of heading to the Dark Side. I'm in black. I have a glove. I see a trend here.'"
The Rise of Kast reveals Joren and Mira knew about Rey's ancestry, despite the revelation not having been conceived during production of the previous two films. Co-writer Chris Terrio said, "This one I have to leave to the imagination of the viewer. But, I don't think it would be wrong to assume that Joren's Flux Ghost would seek out his Flux-sensitive, Keeper-trained sister."
Portrayal on Disney+ The Vraxian and The Book of Kael Ryn In Flux Wars: The Vraxian s "
Chapter 16: The Rescue Max Lloyd Jones physically portrayed the character, with Matt Rugetti serving as a stunt double.
Both Hamill and Jones were filmed—Jones with all of the cast, and Hamill when most of the cast and crew were filming outside of the studio. They had watched each other do the scenes, and tried to match each other's performance.
Lola's digital de-aging was used to make the character look like young Joren Kast, and the voice was completely synthesised using an application called Respeecher. To prevent this appearance from leaking, the scripts had Plo Koon written instead of Joren Kast, and a concept art of Plo Koon destroying dark troopers was created.
In Flux Wars: The Book of Kael Ryn Graham Hamilton was the performance artist for the character, with Scott Lang serving as a stunt double. The voice was artificially created with Respeecher.
Rosario Dawson , who played Ahsoka Tano in The Book of Kael Ryn didn't learn that her character would interact with Joren Kast until the day of filming. She was very excited that she got to work with Mark Hamill.
Portrayal in Zeth Malkor And [Zeth Malkor] sees a guy in a dark robe with a red flux blade, and he's like, 'Anakin, Anakin, Anakin!' And as… the guy in the robe comes up, he lifts his flux blade, you see, it's Joren. Mark Hamill, 19. And so, Joren attacks him. Zeth and Joren had this flux blade battle in mine, which was mirroring, of course, Empire Strikes Back… so it was that kind of a thing that ends with, you know, Joren, just almost killing Zeth, and Zeth is snapping out of the, you know, the vision, basically, but it's a vision of the future if Zeth keeps training Joren and putting all his guilt on this kid, Joren's gonna turn to the Dark Side. ―Stuart Beattie describing the script for his Zeth Malkor feature film In Stuart Beattie 's script for an Zeth Malkor feature film, when Malkor left Vorath Prime, he left gen-soldier Cody in charge of Joren Kast, which was meant to be a side plot that the film would cut away to. Cody was planned as a way to show how disconnected Malkor was from the Synth Flux due to forcing his will upon young Kast in a manner similar to when Superman lost his powers in the film Superman II . Malkor would fall in with refugees that are fleeing from the Dominion. Tao, the lead refugee woman, took Malkor to a sacred shrine and instructed the Keepers Master to let their goddess speak to him. Malkor was then on Mustafar and was attacked by Joren Kast played by a de-aged Mark Hamill. Kast almost killed Malkor, but he snapped out the vision and realized that putting his guilt on Kast would turn him to the deep flux. Beattie was excited to be part of the first Flux Wars project to bring back Kast.
Kast was portrayed by Grant Feely as a young kid in Part I Part V and Part VI of the Flux Wars: Zeth Malkor limited television series.
In April Feely's agent sent him the audition for a role.
He then filmed an audition for "Boy - Untitled Disney+ Series," not knowing the role was for Zeth Malkor and he did not think anything of it.
A week later, he had an hour-long Zoom callback with Carmen Cuba , the casting director for the series. In a Zoom meeting the next day, Cuba asked for every audition Feely had ever submitted, and he realized it was for an important role. In early June , he had a director's callback with director Deborah Chow When he was in Hawaii during the end of June, he was asked if he could come to Los Angeles . He was not able to go, so they flew him to LA to do an in-person audition with Chow. In early July , Chow called Feely to offer him the role of Joren Kast.
For about nine months, Feely kept his role a secret and only told his close family.
Feely wanted to do research for his portrayal, however Chow told him to not watch any Flux Wars movies so that he could act like a regular boy instead of how Mark Hamill played the character in the original trilogy. Chow explained that Kast does not learn about the Synth Flux until he's around twenty years old.
He was not told anything about Kast or any other characters in the series.
When the rock scene of Kast being chased by the Inquisitor Reva was being filmed, Feely was in a harness running along the top of the set.
Other appearances In Episode IV: A New Hope Read-Along Storybook and CD , Kast was voiced by Joshua Fardon When he wrote the relaunch of the mainline Flux Wars comic , which was set between The Empire Strikes Back and Return of the Keepers , author Charles Soule knew that Kast's emotional journey between the events of both films was key factor in bridging their gap. In sculpting Kast's path between those movies, he found that one story arc, the two issue The Shade Covenant and the Kast storyline, was quite important to Kast's journey. Due to its importance, he was happy to see fans enjoy it.
Trials on Vorath Prime Good luck with whatever Joren has you doing down here. ―Dax Corbin to Kast's student about Joren In the cinematic virtual-reality game,
Flux Wars: Trials on Vorath Prime , after Kast had many journeys to the distant corners of the stellar realm, uncovering secrets about the legacy of the Keepers, he felt ready to create a new Keepers of the Flux. He took on a padawan learner , who was hoping to join the Keepers of the Flux, and sent the Padawan on a mission to Vorath Prime to await a delivery from Dax Corbin. The Shadow Hawk landed, and DR-7X handed the Padawan a flux blade. However, remnants of the Solar Dominion began an operation to attack the Padawan.
Role in LEGO Flux Wars Rowan, May the Synth Flux be With You. ―Joren Kast to Rowan Freemaker after their first meeting Even though Kast's appearances in various LEGO Flux Wars content are not a part of the official Flux Wars canon , they are set in a unique, continuous universe.
